10 Reasons You Will Be Playing GTA 6 For More Than a Decade

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ronald is a video game journalist with more than 10 years of experience in the industry, working on several of the most important Spanish websites.

After a week of GTA 6's availability, there are two things I can say with complete certainty: first, Red Dead Redemption 2 is the best thing that could have happened to Rockstar, and second, we'll be following Jason, Lucia, and company for a long time.

The developer's magnum opus offers an immersive and expansive world that will take us many years to fully decipher, so I can already imagine how numerous ecosystems, from video games to content creation, will be completely permeated until, perhaps, the release of the seventh installment.

However, far from wanting to theorize about future adventures of the IP, the priority is to appreciate the one we already have in our hands and savor it slowly, since that's what the game itself aims for by offering a story, mechanics, and context that evolve at a much more leisurely pace.

Therefore, in order to play fortune teller and anticipate how long we will be hanging around Leonida, I invite you to read this list of ten reasons you will be playing GTA 6 for more than a decade.

Analyzing the evolution of video games, especially considering their affinity with technology, is an activity that involves intertemporal comparisons, and GTA 6 has established itself as the generational benchmark we look to when we want to see if the industry has moved forward.

Titles like The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, BioShock, The Elder Scrolls V: Skyrim, and The Last of Us Part II have stood for years as monoliths of their respective genres and platforms, garnering enduring attention that regularly draws us back to reaffirm their influence.

Thus, considering the technical, graphical, and design marvel that is Rockstar's title, particularly within the context of open worlds, it will be impossible for it to escape associations with future representatives of the genre, who will undoubtedly seek to learn from it.

Although comparisons are generally odious, it's also true that they constitute a timely method to contrast the evolution, or involution, of a medium, so I can already see many scholars of this art revisiting its confines to diagnose its eventual state.

While I acknowledge the omnipresence of the internet and our hyper-connected state has made the task of hunting for secrets virtually impossible, I know I'm not the only one who has deliberately avoided all discussion about the game in order to discover it for myself.

By choosing not to use guides, watch gameplay videos, or even compare opinions, the game's lifespan is instantly extended due to its immeasurable size and content, much of which is hidden behind hundreds of hours of spontaneous and meticulous exploration.

You may finish the campaign and feel satisfied with what you've experienced, but for a game that has taken so long to arrive and generated so much anticipation, I'm sure that a large portion of its player base will continue playing until there's no dialogue, NPC, mission, weapon, or random event left undiscovered.

This largely depends on how much you choose to interact with its online discourse, as you will surely encounter spoilers of all kinds as the days and months go by, yet a whole second life awaits you if you choose to turn a blind eye to other people's experiences and strive to discover everything on your own.

Assuming I'm not the only oddball who's spent countless hours wandering around open-world games listening to their tunes without engaging in any particular activity, GTA 6's deep and outstanding soundtrack will behave like a music player with extra steps.

In fact, a large part of my GTA 5 playtime consisted of countless nights spent hopping into any legitimately acquired vehicle and cruising around the city's interiors and exteriors, and after just an hour of playing this new installment, I already knew the future would be the same.

Whether you enjoy pop, urban music, rap, or listening to the radio as if it were real life (which it is closer to than I'd like to admit), there's a station for every type of listener, helping the game also become an album that will remind you of the era in which it was released.

Never underestimate the power of songs as tools to prolong our connection with a video game because, just like with Secunda in Skyrim or I Really Want To Stay at Your House in Cyberpunk 2077, I'm sure RHYNO will have us going around in circles in Vice City for a very, very long time.

Like many games, Grand Theft Auto 6 is full of easter eggs for you to discover. These are some of the best Leonida has to offer.

A major surprise I encountered in my first few hours of playing GTA 6 was the influence of immersive sims on its mission design, as there isn't just one way to solve many of the main and side objectives in the campaign.

Obviously, we're not talking about the depth of an Arkane Studios title, though there's enough density and player agency to allow for diverse approaches, ranging from peaceful resolutions if you know who to talk to, to stealthy sieges where no one necessarily has to be killed.

Add to that the game's inherent spontaneity, which throws you into special and unexpected encounters every second, all waiting to grab your attention, and you realize its replayability stems from a design specifically intended to be open and multifaceted.

I hope GTA 6's approach helps the genre emerge from its slumber, but while I invoke every possible deity to make that happen, I'm content to return to Leonida every few years to see what new interactions Rockstar prepared without me noticing in my first five playthroughs.

In conjunction with the above, the title places a notable emphasis on role-playing elements, influencing both the story and gameplay according to how the player chooses to interact with their world, allowing Jason and Lucia to be canvases with clear forms yet ultimately emphasized by the player.

Between choosing the type of relationship they develop, making choices that influence the narrative at key plot points, and the constant decision-making involved in adhering to (or not) morality and law, you can have completely different experiences if you shift your mindset enough.

In this sense, for my first playthrough, I have a more restrained and rigorous Jason and a more antisocial and rebellious Lucia within a loving relationship, always acting in accordance with these personality archetypes. Regardless, for my second playthrough, I plan to take a completely opposite path.

The result? At the very least, one more run with hundreds of hours and variations that will make me even more amazed by the immeasurable talent of the Rockstar workers, to whom I feel I owe the respect of playing the game at least three times to properly appreciate the countless hours they invested in shaping the product.

The third of my three planned playthroughs, designed to fully exploit the game, will consist of not killing anyone unless the game forces me to, which brings us to the mechanism that has kept hundreds of video games alive online: challenge runs.

Beating the entire game with only fists, without breaking any laws, without using money, with only a knife, using only public transport, in the shortest time feasible, as the worst possible partner... I don't know if the possibilities are truly infinite, but they certainly come close.

Games like Dark Souls and Hollow Knight have demonstrated for years that human creativity knows no bounds, especially when it comes to creating content that borders on masochism, so it's only natural that GTA 6 will be like a second home for all these inventive people.

I don't know if I'll be among them, yet my experience being chronically online assures me with complete certainty that GTA 6 will sustain entire communities based on challenges, rising exponentially if mods are added to the equation.

Some players may think they should register a hot new sports car as their starter car. There's actually a better choice. Here's the best starter car to register in GTA VI.

Heraclitus said it's impossible to step into the same river twice because everything changes, both in the river itself and in the person crossing it, and I believe the mythical Greek philosopher made this aphorism with the belief that we would eventually have the boundless body of water that is GTA 6.

Experiences with open worlds, like the aforementioned Skyrim or, more recently, Elden Ring, tend to be special thanks to the organic nature of their worlds, given you feel like you're in an alternate, digital reality where you're truly part of a larger cosmos.

Leonida, however, takes these sensations to uncharted territory, where it genuinely feels impossible to experience the same thing twice because of how reactive and multifaceted its systems are, like hundreds of thousands of windows that only open if you're in the right place at the right time.

This contextual fertility ensures that there will almost always be a new experience to go through, and as long as the flame of belief that there are unexplored horizons remains alive (something that GTA 5 already demonstrated), there will be parishioners who remain absorbed watching the fire, waiting to hear an unprecedented crackle.

The fear of missing out on a cultural event is fascinating because, contrary to popular belief, it can have a timeless quality; for example, it's the reason I played the first iterations of Red Dead Redemption and The Last of Us, even though it was a decade after their respective releases.

Given GTA 6 is and will be a cultural touchstone of modern entertainment, many will return to its realm years later to satisfy a curiosity they perhaps didn't solve at launch for one reason or another, ranging from waiting for a price drop to the complete optimization of its features.

Beyond the causes, and considering a legacy that is already titanic after just one week of release and will become even more staggering as time goes on, it seems that playing GTA 6 will be like watching The Godfather or reading the Odyssey: an essential ritual for understanding the path its medium will take in the future.

Even if you're not a fan of the series or the genre, the curiosity to understand the reasons behind its multi-million sales and resounding unanimous praise as a masterpiece on Metacritic will be compelling for those who value video games as an art form, since great works always deserve attention, even if it's to criticize them.

A very upset parrot is more than willing to point players in the direction of cash if they beat down its jailers.

Rockstar has found its closest model to follow in GTA 5, the same recipe that allowed them to maintain the product's relevance for over ten years with virtually no significant drop in popularity or impact, whose success hinges on spacing out re-releases, which will rekindle the unresolved desires of a significant portion of the gaming community.

Motivated by the release of the PC version and potential upcoming consoles from Microsoft and Sony, or even by the anticipation of a product with as few bugs and inconsistencies as possible, many people will refrain from jumping on the bandwagon at its peak, instead patiently waiting for the waters to calm down.

The 235 million copies sold of its predecessor didn't happen overnight; they materialized gradually and steadily year after year, and it's quite easy to predict that market behavior this time around will be virtually the same.

Many of them are repeat buyers, of course, but that doesn't invalidate the title's statement: whether it's your first time or your fiftieth, the hardware will give you very appealing reasons to discover or check out how GTA 6 is, even if it's 2036.

However, by far, the biggest reason to be sure you'll be playing GTA 6 for more than ten years lies in its multiplayer mode, a fundamental pillar that sustained GTA 5 throughout its lifespan and which, without a doubt, will repeat that feat with this new installment.

If we assume Rockstar will be able to create a product that surpasses its predecessor as the base game did, the only sensible conclusion about the next iteration of GTA Online is that it will be a candidate for the most massive, complex, and profitable multiplayer experience of all time.

Phenomena like Fortnite or League of Legends cast doubt on such a claim, but the fact that this is even the scale used to measure GTA 6's multiplayer is indicative enough of what it will mean for the franchise and the market once it's released.

More than the sheer scale of its world, more than sociocultural pressure, and more than even any traditional DLC, what could allow Rockstar to go two generations without releasing a game and still be the most popular developer in the world is the online variant of GTA 6.

Not only because of its name and legacy, which also mean a great deal, but because of the game's inherent quality, which suggests that its size, freedom of action, and overall quality will be remarkable enough to carry the Grand Theft Auto name for as long as it lasts.

Grand Theft Auto 6 puts a large emphasis on keeping your relationship sound, so you need to know what ways are the best to raise the romance between Jason and Lucia.

Share your take with fellow players. Keep it constructive, relevant, and respectful.

New York Governor Kathy Hochul signs ban on AI chatbot toys backed by $15,000 daily fines

The ban comes into force in 90 days, leaving parents at risk of buying AI chatbot toys this holiday season.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

New York Governor Kathy Hochul has signed a bill that effectively bans the sale of toys with built-in AI chatbots within the state over concerns about what they might say to kids. But the ban will come too late for this Holiday season, leaving parents at risk of buying toys without realizing the risks that they could pose to their children. The ban will come into effect 90 days from Tuesday, November 24, WXXI News reports, and targets toys like stuffed animals, dolls, and robots that use a microphone and speaker to allow children to communicate with a relatively rudimentary chatbot. But with safeguards often lacking, it can be difficult to predict how these toys will behave and what they will say to young children who might not realize what they are speaking to.

New York's ban comes after similar moves by other states around the United States, with California already announcing a four-year moratorium on the sale and manufacturing of AI toys to children under 16. Pennsylvania is also considering similar action. Back in New York, Hochul has hinted that more AI safety proposals are in the works for 2027. “We'll set clear safety standards and put independent experts inside these companies to make sure that they're following the rules, and that we'll insist that humans remain in control, and with plans to contain the danger if something does go wrong,” the governor said. There are a few ways that could happen, with State Senator Andrew Gournardes (D-Brooklyn) noting that a new Common Sense Media Your AI Safety Institute report found AI chatbot toys can undermine a child's relationships with their family and friends.

The ban will hit toy manufacturers, sellers, and operators with fines of $15,000 per day if they are found in violation. It also follows similar tech-focused child safety laws, such as requiring age verification for social media for anyone under the age of 18. Warning labels must also be present. It's possible that Hochul will look to expand that requirement to AI chatbots, including those not built into toys.

Alarm over AI chatbots is nothing new, of course, but the new wave of devices aimed at children is causing concern. Beyond the impact on children, AI psychosis continues to be an issue, with a June 2025 report finding that OpenAI's ChatGPT encouraged dangerous and untrue beliefs about a range of things, including trying to convince one user that they were Neo from the popular The Matrix movie franchise. Some companies have worked to make their products safer, with limited success. OpenAI released a new version of its ChatGPT chatbot that would be less sycophantic and push back against users more, only for those users to revolt over the move. OpenAI later rolled the change back.

Monster Must Die is a new roguelike tower defence from Habby now in pre-registration on Android

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Habby is a developer and publisher that has released some neat little mobile games over the years. You may recall Archero One and Two, as well as Dicero and Capybara Go! Now, Habby are once more tackling the roguelike genre, and fusing it with tower defence as pre-registration opens for Monster Must Die!

Monster Must Die, as mentioned above, mashes up two genres with tower defence and roguelike. You start each battle with a summoning tower, then gradually introduce up to four heroes to fight off near-endless waves of monsters.

The world could always use more heroes Of course, as you might expect, Monster Must Die takes some cues from Vampire Survivors, especially in having the myriad of strategies and skills that you can combo together to take on the forces of evil! That means plenty of opportunities to tinker with builds.

And Habby are also eager to point out you have effectively unlimited stamina, meaning that you won't need to worry about waiting on timers to get back into the fight. You'll certainly need that stamina to take on the hordes and power up by visiting the Dungeon.

Overall, Monster Must Die looks like a lot of Habby's other releases: a perfectly competent take on a tried-and-true genre. About the only thing I can say is that it doesn't do much to stand out otherwise. Hopefully, when it does release, we'll see it making more of an impression, but for now it's just worth keeping an eye on.

A Dependency-Free Before/After Image Slider With clip-path and a Range Input

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Before/after sliders are everywhere: photo editors, retouching portfolios, image tools. Most tutorials reach for a library, but a good one fits in about 40 lines of HTML, CSS and JavaScript using clip-path and a range input. It is accessible, works with touch, and has no dependencies.

Stack two images exactly on top of each other. Show the "after" image fully, and clip the "before" image so only its left part is visible. Moving the slider changes how much of the "before" image you see.

Using a real gives us keyboard support and screen-reader semantics for free.

clip-path: inset(top right bottom left) hides everything to the right of --pos. The range input sits invisibly on top of the whole image, so dragging anywhere moves the divider.

That is the whole behaviour. Everything else is CSS reading one custom property.

Retouching portfolios, image compression comparisons, and tools that clean up images. For instance, a page showing before and after comparison results for corner cleanup on AI images makes the effect obvious in a way two separate thumbnails do not.

No library, no build step, and it degrades to two stacked images if JavaScript fails.

How to Run a Feedback Round That Actually Changes the Product

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Early feedback has a familiar shape. You show someone your product, they say "looks great, I'd definitely use it," and you leave feeling encouraged and no wiser.

That isn't because people are lying. Most are being kind, and kindness is the enemy of information. A useful feedback round is designed to get past politeness and into behavior: what people do, what confuses them, and what they'd actually give up time or money for.

Here's a simple process we'd use with a small team and a small number of users. It doesn't need special tools.

Feedback without a question is a pile of opinions. Before you talk to anyone, write down one to three things you're unsure about. For example:

Each uncertainty shapes who you ask and what you ask. "Do they understand it?" needs fresh eyes. "Do they have the problem?" needs people from your target audience. Mixing them gives muddled answers.

Five to eight people from your real audience will teach you more than fifty from anywhere. Friends and family are comfortable to ask and unreliable to learn from; they want you to succeed.

Aim for variety within the audience: a newer user and a more experienced one, a skeptical person and an enthusiastic one.

The most reliable method is also the simplest: give someone a realistic task and watch silently.

Five people stumbling on the same step is a clear signal, and nothing you'd get from a questionnaire shows that as vividly. You'll often find that the thing you assumed was obvious is the thing everyone misses.

People are poor at predicting their own behavior. "Would you use this?" invites a hopeful yes. "Would you pay for it?" invites an optimistic yes. Both are cheap to give.

Questions about actual past behavior are harder to fake:

If someone has no current workaround and has never looked for one, the problem might not be painful enough. If they've built a spreadsheet and three reminders to cope, you're near something real.

Avoid leading questions, such as "Don't you think this is easier?" Ask neutral ones: "How did that compare with what you do now?" And when you've asked, stop talking. Silence is the most effective follow-up question.

During and after each conversation, mark what stands out:

Be cautious with polite compliments. "Nice, clean design" is pleasant and tells you almost nothing. "Can I keep using this next week?" is a much better sign.

After the round, put everything in one place. A simple table or a wall of notes works:

The aim isn't statistical certainty. With a handful of people, you're looking for clear, repeated patterns, and being honest that this is a small sample.

Not all feedback deserves action. A way to sort it:

Resist the urge to build everything people ask for. A product assembled from every suggestion tends to serve no one in particular. Your job is to understand the need behind the request, then choose the simplest way to meet it, or decide not to.

People who give feedback remember it. A short message afterward makes a difference:

It costs little and builds the kind of relationship that makes the next round easier to run.

You don't need a research department. A rhythm that works for a small team:

Small, regular rounds beat one big study, because you learn while you can still change direction cheaply.

What's the one question you'd most like a real user to answer about your product?

We're nothink, a small team that designs and builds products for startups — more at nothink.site.

Building a Planar-Graph Floor Plan Room Solver in Pure TypeScript

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 26 • Originally published at plancaliber.hashnode.dev

When a user drags walls on a 2D canvas—or uploads a hand-drawn sketch that gets parsed into approximate wall segments—you do not start with clean room polygons. You start with a messy soup of line segments:

If you try to run polygon detection straight on raw wall segments, room areas vanish, doors float off their host walls, and exterior square footage breaks. You do not need a multi-megabyte WebAssembly CAD kernel to solve this cleanly in the browser. A deterministic planar-graph pipeline in pure TypeScript handles the whole topology problem in under a millisecond on typical residential plans.

Here is the exact architecture we built in src/lib/cad/healer.ts for PlanCaliber, our browser-based 2D/3D floor plan CAD engine.

Before extracting a single room polygon, we run a deterministic four-pass healer (healPlanDocument) on a cloned wall graph. Order matters—welding before splitting prevents near-coincident corners from creating microscopic triangles when T-junctions get projected:

In Pass 1 (weldVerticesInPlace), vertices within weldRadiusIn (default 8" for rough sketch imports, tighter for direct CAD drags) are grouped using a disjoint-set (Union-Find) with path compression.

A subtle trap when merging a cluster of vertices is taking a blind arithmetic mean. If three existing CAD vertices already sit cleanly at x = 144.000" and one sloppy hand-drawn endpoint lands at x = 146.500", averaging drags all four vertices to 144.625", nudging perpendicular walls out of square.

Instead, we bucket candidate coordinates at 0.05" precision first:

If two or more vertices already agree within 0.05", that exact coordinate wins and the stray vertex snaps to it. Only when every vertex in the cluster disagrees do we fall back to a quarter-inch snapped mean.

When an interior partition terminates within 6" of an exterior wall's span, we project the endpoint perpendicularly onto the host wall, insert a shared vertex, and split the host wall into two child segments.

Because doors and windows are stored parametrically (wallId + offsetIn measured from startId), splitting a wall must remap every attached opening onto the child segment that now contains its center—recomputing offsetIn relative to the new split vertex—and Pass 4 (clampOpeningsInPlace) ensures no door jamb overlaps a mitered corner (>= 3" margin from both ends).

Once every intersection and T-junction shares a vertex, the wall graph is planar—except for dangling wing walls, kitchen peninsulas, and open closets whose endpoints have degree 1.

If you leave degree-1 edges in the graph while tracing half-edge faces, the traversal walks down the dangling wall, spins 180 degrees around the dead end, and walks back along its twin—producing degenerate slit polygons that ruin label placement and interior offset geometry.

Before creating half-edges, we iteratively prune every wall touching a degree-1 vertex (a standard 2-core peel):

Crucially, the pruned walls are not deleted from the plan document—they still render in 2D, extrude in 3D, appear in wall stud/drywall takeoffs, and export to AutoCAD R12 .DXF. They are simply omitted from the closed-face cycle extractor.

Every surviving undirected wall becomes two directed half-edges (A -> B and B -> A) linked by twinId, storing their outgoing angle Math.atan2(dy, dx).

At every vertex, we sort all outgoing half-edges in ascending angular order. Then, starting from each unvisited half-edge cur:

In screen/canvas coordinates (+x right, +y down), stepping one index backward from the incoming twin always chooses the sharpest interior turn, walking strictly around the boundary of a single minimal face:

Notice how elegantly the Shoelace signed area solves the "outer perimeter" problem in a single line:

Getting the basic half-edge walk right is only half the story. Residential plans immediately hit three practical geometry edge cases:

Walls have physical thickness—typically 6" for exterior framed walls and 4.5" for interior 2×4 stud partitions.

Separating these three measurements in the solver—rather than fudging one number—is why you can simultaneously get accurate interior drywall/flooring takeoffs and a real exterior-face GLA total. (We also broke the standalone ANSI rules out into a free ANSI Z765 Gross Living Area Calculator.)

If a homeowner draws a freestanding pantry or fireplace chase completely inside an open-concept great room without walls connecting it to the outer shell, the half-edge extractor yields two separate closed cycles: the pantry and the great room.

Using point-in-polygon checks across extracted faces (findEnclosingParentIndices), we detect when cycle B sits strictly inside cycle A, subtract B's footprint from A's net floor area, and pass B's polygon as a hole when placing A's room label.

A polygon's naive centroid frequently lands outside an L-shaped hallway or right on top of an island hole. ensureInteriorLabelPoint(poly, holes) tests the centroid first; if it falls outside the polygon, inside a hole, or too close to a wall, it sweeps interior grid candidates and picks the point maximizing minimum distance to every boundary and hole edge—so room tags and 12'-4" × 15'-8" callouts always sit in open floor space.

This entire healer, planar face extractor, clearance auditor, 3D cutaway extruder, pdf-lib scaled PDF writer, and zero-dependency AutoCAD R12 .DXF serializer runs live in PlanCaliber—no account required to draw, drag walls, calibrate a sketch, and watch rooms re-solve in real time.

If you want to inspect the downstream CAD artifact generated directly from the solved wall & room graph, you can download a live unwatermarked specimen here: Sample AutoCAD R12 .DXF Specimen (pure ASCII AC1009, $INSUNITS = 1 inches across 8 AIA layers).

(All PlanCaliber outputs are for preliminary space planning and drafting—always verify dimensions and local building codes on site.)

Scaloni, altro Mondiale! Il CT dell’Argentina si qualifica nella Gran Fondo di ciclismo

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Un altro Mondiale per Lionel Scaloni, questa volta lontano dalla panchina e dal pallone. Il commissario tecnico dell'Argentina ha conquistato la qualificazione ai Campionati del Mondo UCI Gran Fondo 2027 grazie al terzo posto ottenuto nella categoria M45-49 alla Gran Fondo Siete Lagos, disputata in Patagonia. Una prova di 110 chilometri completata in 2 ore, 51 minuti e 54 secondi, risultato che gli ha consegnato il pass per la rassegna iridata in programma in Francia.

Il tecnico campione del mondo con l'Argentina a Qatar 2022 ha preso parte alla 15ª edizione della manifestazione, che collega San Martín de los Andes a Villa La Angostura, lungo la suggestiva Ruta Nacional 40. Con il pettorale numero 897, Scaloni ha chiuso al 40° posto nella classifica assoluta, ma soprattutto sul terzo gradino del podio nella propria fascia d'età, quella riservata ai corridori tra i 45 e i 49 anni. Davanti a lui, nella categoria composta da 179 partecipanti, soltanto altri due concorrenti. Un risultato gli ha infatti garantito l'accesso al Mondiale Gran Fondo del 2027, appuntamento riservato agli atleti qualificati attraverso le prove del circuito internazionale UCI.

La passione di Scaloni per il ciclismo non rappresenta una novità. Dopo aver chiuso la carriera da calciatore, l'allenatore argentino ha trovato nella bicicletta una nuova dimensione sportiva, trasformando progressivamente un interesse personale in un'attività praticata con costanza e spirito competitivo: "Da quando ho smesso di giocare a calcio, mi sono appassionato al ciclismo. Mi alleno per un'ora e mezza ogni giorno", aveva raccontato il commissario tecnico.

Il prossimo appuntamento internazionale è previsto nell'Alta Savoia, in Francia, dove dal 22 al 24 agosto 2027 si svolgeranno le prove del Mondiale UCI Gran Fondo. In programma una cronometro individuale di 12,7 chilometri, il 22 agosto, e la prova principale di 115 chilometri, il 24 agosto, tra Thônes e La Clusaz, con oltre 3.200 metri di dislivello.

4 Christmas gift options: 3 I've tested and 1 I'm excited to try soon.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Like lots of parents, I'm always looking for activities that stop my kids sitting sedentary in front of screens. I use all the tech-free tactics I can, like arts and crafts and board games, but I also value the experiences tech can give them. I just want that tech to be more creative than a tablet or the TV.

Not all kids' tech is evil, and some is pretty darn great. I've found a niche that combines being a parent, my love of design and my years as an early years teacher, and it means I get to test some seriously good products. There are a few I'm firmly recommending, and they'd make great Christmas presents.

I love the Yoto. I can't shout about this enough. We've been using it for the last seven years, I bought one when my daughter was two, and it's going from strength to strength in functionality and design (read my interview with the team who recently redesigned the hardware). The Yoto does have a screen but is very deliberately only using it to display fun pixel graphics, and the time.

If you're not familiar, you play stories and music hosted on cards via the Yoto machine – which comes in a full sized and mini version. Cards are available to buy – but you can also make your own cards, which is the major selling point for me. You can record your own cards, or you can put MP3 files onto them. There's also an ever-changing library of podcasts and other audio, accessed via an app.

Yoto is currently up to 30% off – and it would make a fabulous Christmas present.

I bang on about the Nex Playground to everyone – my family adores it. It's an active play system that gets players up and moving through its whole body tracking. In my experience, this console counters the usual effect video games or tablets have on children. It encourages discussion, collaboration with its gentle and imaginative games, which include sports like Bowling (my son's favourite), and big-name titles like How to Train Your Dragon and Bluey. See our full Nex Playground review here.

If you're thinking about a console for your children this Christmas, I really can't recommend this one enough – and it's also currently got 37% in the UK, and 30% in the US off for Black Friday.

If you've got a younger child interested in 3D printing, this is a great option. As I say in my Funbox review, if your child is at the upper end of the 4-12 age bracket, I'd go for a proper 3D printer. My daughter is on the borderline at 9, but my son (5) would get plenty of life from this printer.

It's a fun design, and is super-simple to use via an app. You can draw designs and let the AI print them, or pick from a marketplace of designs (make sure you use the official UGEE designs for maximum effectiveness). Because it's enclosed, it's safer for kids to use.

It's currently got a small saving of $20 at Amazon.

This is the one I'm yet to try, but I'm so excited by the idea. I join a lot of parents in not wanting my children to have their own phones for a long while yet, but I realised that when I was younger I would be phoning my friends on the landline. We don't have landline, and I gatekeep my own phone for obvious reasons. So I was searching for a solution – and the Tin Can is perfect.

Pitched as a New-School Landline phone, it connects to the WiFi and only phones/accepts calls from approved numbers (controlled via an app). If another child has a TinCan, they can phone each other for free, and other approved numbers can be called if you pay a monthly subscription.

I've got one on its way to me so I'll be doing a review on it soon, but I wanted to put it on your radar as I think it's such a smart idea. It's out in the US, and preorders are open for before-Christmas delivery in the UK.

AI Engineer vs Machine Learning Engineer vs AI Automation Engineer

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 26 • Originally published at barcelonacodeschool.com

A machine learning engineer builds and operates models and ML pipelines. An AI engineer usually builds applications and systems that use models, retrieval, agents or other AI services. An AI automation engineer connects existing models, business data and software tools into controlled workflows. The titles overlap, so choose by the work described: model ownership, product/application ownership, or business-process ownership.

Ask what the engineer is primarily responsible for keeping correct. A machine learning engineer owns the path from data to a trained, served and monitored model. An AI engineer owns a product or application that uses AI capabilities. An AI automation engineer owns a business workflow in which AI is one decision-making component among databases, SaaS tools, rules and human approvals.

These boundaries are practical, not official. A small company may call one person an AI engineer while expecting all three kinds of work. A larger organisation may split model training, AI product development and business integration across separate teams.

Accessible text equivalent: ML engineers centre the model lifecycle; AI engineers centre an AI-enabled application; AI automation engineers centre an end-to-end business process with integrations and human control.

A machine learning engineer turns data and modelling work into a reliable production capability. Current Google Cloud and AWS role guides include data preparation, model or foundation-model development, training, serving, pipeline orchestration, monitoring, security and ongoing improvement.

This path fits people who enjoy mathematics, experiments, data quality and infrastructure as much as model output. Typical projects include demand forecasting, recommendation, classification, computer vision, model fine-tuning or a reusable inference service. The engineer must explain metrics, data leakage, generalisation, drift and the operational cost of serving.

In this comparison, AI engineer means the application-focused role. The engineer selects or integrates models, builds retrieval and agent logic, connects the system to a product, evaluates task behaviour and operates the application. Microsoft’s current AI-103 profile names planning, generative and agentic solutions, information extraction, security and cross-functional collaboration.

The role still needs software engineering. A model call without authentication, schemas, tests, permissions, monitoring or failure handling is not a finished application. Some employers use “AI engineer” as an umbrella that includes ML engineering, so the vacancy must confirm the boundary.

An AI automation engineer begins with a business process: lead intake, document handling, reporting, knowledge access, customer requests or internal operations. The engineer connects triggers, models, databases and business applications, then adds rules, approvals, logs and recovery.

The model should not control permissions or consequential decisions. External messages, payments, access changes and sensitive actions remain behind deterministic rules or human approval. No-code platforms can reduce programming requirements, but they do not remove the need for data contracts, API knowledge, testing and operational judgement.

These are transition patterns, not hiring guarantees. Prior experience remains valuable because it supplies domain judgement, but each target still needs technical evidence.

Yes in a small team or narrow product, but the combined scope is large. One engineer might fine-tune a model, expose it through an API and connect it to an internal workflow. That does not make the disciplines identical; it means the job is hybrid.

Ask what depth is actually required. If the role trains distributed models, builds customer-facing services and owns enterprise process change, it may be describing several jobs. Clarify priorities, support from data/platform teams and what success means in the first months.

For model and ML engineering, Barcelona Code School’s Deep Learning and AI Engineering Bootcamp teaches Python, data, classical ML, deep learning, Hugging Face, RAG and deployment. The verified live programme is 13 weeks, part-time and remotely mentored. Current tuition is €3,950.

For AI automation engineering, the AI Agent & Automation Bootcamp teaches n8n, APIs, data, RAG, tools, approvals, QA, orchestration and deployment. The verified live programme is four weeks/160 hours, on campus or remote live. Current tuition is €3,600.

For an application-focused AI engineer, choose according to your missing layer: experienced developers may need the AI-system layer; beginners targeting model or software-heavy roles need deeper programming foundations. Neither course guarantees employment.

Not always. Machine learning engineers centre data, models, training, serving and MLOps. AI engineers often build applications with models, retrieval or agents. Some employers use AI engineer as an umbrella title, so the responsibilities matter more than the label.

Not every project requires advanced programming because visual orchestration tools can handle much of the workflow. The role still needs logic, structured data, APIs, authentication, testing and debugging. Code becomes useful when integrations or controls exceed platform features.

Machine learning engineering usually requires the deepest statistics, probability and linear algebra because the role owns model development and evaluation. AI application and automation roles need strong evaluation and data literacy but may use existing models.

The best route depends on transferable experience. Operations, product and consulting backgrounds often fit AI automation; software developers often fit AI applications; data science and quantitative backgrounds often fit ML engineering. Projects must close the technical gap.

Yes. Add stronger programming, backend architecture, testing, deployment and deeper model/retrieval knowledge. The transition is credible when portfolio projects move from visual workflows to maintainable AI services or applications.

Choose AI application engineering for code-first agents inside software products. Choose AI automation engineering for agents that coordinate business tools and workflows. Choose ML engineering when your main responsibility is training or adapting the models behind them.

Zcash Whale Wakes Up After 9 Years, Moves $11.39 Million To Kraken

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Nine years is an eternity in crypto. Yet one Zcash whale appears to have spent that long sitting on an investment made during the privacy coin’s early days. On Thursday, blockchain monitoring platform Lookonchain reported that the investor had moved 15,750 ZEC, worth $11.39 million, to Kraken.

The coins date back to 2017, when Ethereum was still a relatively young network, decentralized finance barely existed and Zcash was attempting to establish itself as a more private alternative to Bitcoin.

Almost a decade later, the wallet is active again. And its owner’s reported acquisition price makes the transaction particularly interesting.

According to Lookonchain, the whale originally acquired 23,412 ZEC at an average price of approximately $215, representing an investment of just over $5 million.

The latest transactions account for roughly two-thirds of that original position, with at least 7,750 ZEC transferred to a Kraken deposit address.

At the reported transfer valuation of approximately $723 per ZEC, those 15,750 coins are worth around $8 million more than their estimated acquisition cost. That would represent a return of approximately 236% if the investor sold them at that price.

What makes this wallet fascinating, however, is what happened between those two valuations.

Zcash spent much of 2024 and early 2025 trading around $16 to $20 before staging a remarkable comeback. At $20 per coin, the whale’s original investment would have been worth less than $470,000, a paper loss of more than 90%, assuming the position remained intact.

Holding an asset through a temporary market correction is one thing. Holding it through years of declining relevance, while newer cryptocurrencies attract developers, investors and attention, is something else entirely.

Whatever the owner’s reasons for keeping the coins untouched, the decision has aged considerably better than it appeared to during Zcash’s quieter years.

There is something almost ironic about the fact that investors are following the movements of a whale holding one of crypto’s best-known privacy coins.

Unlike networks where transaction privacy is mandatory, Zcash supports both transparent and shielded transactions. Transparent addresses allow observers to examine balances and transfers, while shielded transactions use zero-knowledge cryptography to conceal transaction details.

That distinction explains how blockchain monitoring platforms can identify certain large ZEC movements despite the cryptocurrency’s privacy capabilities.

It also explains why the available information tells only part of the story.

Lookonchain can identify an apparent exchange deposit and estimate its value. Establishing who controls the wallet, whether ownership changed privately during those nine years or what the investor intends to do next is considerably harder.

Even the reported acquisition price is an estimate rather than a verified record of the owner’s personal trading history.

That is an important limitation of on-chain analysis, particularly when dealing with a network explicitly designed to give users greater control over their financial visibility.

The renewed enthusiasm surrounding Zcash has attracted a different kind of investor from the one buying ZEC in 2017.

Corporate treasury strategies have emerged, institutional investors have taken an interest in privacy-focused assets, and the debate over financial surveillance has given Zcash a fresh investment narrative.

NewsBTC previously reported that Winklevoss Capital backed the launch of a dedicated Zcash treasury strategy, illustrating how exposure to the cryptocurrency has begun extending beyond traditional crypto wallets.

But the revival of an old cryptocurrency also comes with an interesting complication: investors who have spent years underwater finally have an opportunity to recover their capital or take profits.

That does not necessarily make the Kraken deposit bearish. A transfer to an exchange is not proof of a completed sale, and one wallet cannot establish a broader trend among Zcash holders.

Still, the transaction is worth watching. If more early investors start moving dormant coins onto exchanges, Zcash’s new buyers may find themselves absorbing supply from an entirely different generation of holders.

Nine years ago, someone reportedly committed more than $5 million to the idea of private digital money. Today, the blockchain offers a glimpse of what that investment has become.

Whether the owner has finished holding Zcash is a question the blockchain alone cannot yet answer.

ICAO Hosts Air Transport Conference, Elects New Council Members

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Edited By: Vanni Gibertini

Last week, ICAO Headquarters in Montréal hosted the Seventh Worldwide Air Transport Conference, where Member States and industry stakeholders gathered to set global policy priorities on aviation economic regulation, infrastructure financing, resilience, consumer protection and sustainable growth.

Over 1,500 delegates from 154 Member States and organizations attended the week-long event thirteen years after the Sixth Conference in 2013. They discussed high-level topics in commercial aviation: modernizing economic and legal frameworks, investing in infrastructure and skilled workforces, and ensuring that innovation keeps pace with rapidly changing technologies and global demands.

“Broad agreement was reached on the need for harmonized, risk-based, and proportionate economic regulation that facilitates the adoption of emerging technologies such as artificial intelligence, automation, sustainable aviation fuels, and advanced air mobility solutions,” ICAO said in a press release during the Conference.

Participants agreed on the value of frameworks that support innovation and investment for economic development. They stressed this especially for smaller landlocked States, which depend heavily on air connectivity for growth.

The Conference supported modernizing air navigation infrastructure in line with ICAO’s Global Air Navigation Plan. It also supported liberalizing air services agreements without making liberalization conditional on infrastructure development, since new air links fostered by more liberal agreements are seen as key to economic growth.

However, all initiatives should be flexible enough to address local circumstances and should not burden developing States. ICAO’s statement continued: “The Conference also reaffirmed that international standards are key to building effective, market-enabling policies and avoiding fragmentation that could impede cross-border air services. Delegates pointed to ICAO’s Carbon Offsetting and Reduction Scheme for International Aviation (CORSIA) as a successful example of this approach.”

Participants were asked to express their views on the documentation proposing various initiatives with regards to consumer protection legislations that have become an integral part of the legal framework for all air operators.

At the Sixth Worldwide Air Transport Conference in March 2013, ICAO was asked to develop Core Principles on consumer protection. The final document, adopted by the ICAO Council in 2015, contains provisions on:

The report for the Seventh Conference invites States to modernize their consumer-protection frameworks, keeping them evidence-based, proportionate, accessible and responsive to the changing realities of commercial air transport. It also says legislation should establish clear responsibilities for carriers, intermediaries and other providers regarding information, complaints and disruption assistance.

ICAO is expected to review its Core Principles while preserving national and regional flexibility. It is also expected to assess whether existing policies adequately cover online intermediaries and other overlooked situations.

Many Member States cautioned against the ICAO Air Passenger Handbook proposed by IATA, because producing and maintaining a single repository covering many different national laws would be too costly.

The Conference asked ICAO to “examine the need for harmonized, risk-based guidance on the use of AI in aviation, including common terminology and meaningful human oversight.”

The rapid development of AI and its unknown effects on aviation operations is a topic Member States will need to monitor. ICAO will coordinate and facilitate international exchange, while States will oversee implementation to ensure that the technologies adopted are secure, interoperable and consistent with cybersecurity and data-protection objectives.

ICAO will handle AI within its existing structure, as the proposal to create an ad hoc unit was not supported.

During the final two days of the Conference, ICAO held an Extraordinary Session of its Assembly to elect the four additional Council members required by the entry into force of the 2013 protocol amending the Convention on International Civil Aviation. The Council is the Organization’s permanent governing body, accountable to the Assembly and responsible for setting priorities and implementing its directions. Its membership increased from 36 to 40, and there are no permanent seats.

Six candidates competed for the four new seats: Ethiopia, Indonesia, Kazakhstan, Oman, Thailand and Türkiye. In the vote on the final day, Kazakhstan and Thailand were not elected, and the other four States won seats.

The Council now comprises Angola, Argentina, Australia, Belize, Brazil, Canada, China, Colombia, Cuba, Denmark, Ecuador, Egypt, Equatorial Guinea, Ethiopia, France, Germany, India, Indonesia, Italy, Japan, Malaysia, Mali, Mexico, Morocco, Nigeria, Oman, Poland, Qatar, the Republic of Korea, Saudi Arabia, Singapore, South Africa, Spain, Switzerland, Türkiye, Uganda, the United Arab Emirates, the United Kingdom, the United States and Uruguay.

The next elections will take place at the General Assembly in September 2028.

Company was religious about backups but sinned by leaving them exposed

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Never put the backup server in the same Windows domain as production

Welcome, once again, to PWNED, where we’re thankful every time someone leaves a password sticky note on a monitor or fails to delete a terminated employee’s admin account. But neither of those common occurrences is what we’ll be talking about today. Instead, our subject broke the number-one rule of backups: you gotta keep ’em separated.

Our story comes courtesy of Da Li, who serves as global alliance manager at Info2Soft, a company that provides data protection and recovery software. At one point, a partner called him into a case where a Chinese vehicle parts manufacturer fell victim to a ransomware gang. Unfortunately, its backup was compromised too.

The manufacturer had religiously backed up its manufacturing execution system (MES) and SAP orders, inventory, and finance systems every night. There was even a green dashboard that showed the backup process had succeeded, and someone checked that every morning.

However, one Friday evening, crims broke into the network and found out that the backup server was joined to the same Windows domain as the rest of the network. The hackers found a plaintext password for the backup service account in a maintenance script on a file share and discovered that someone had used the very same password for a domain admin account.

So the first thing the crooks did was delete every restore point on that backup server. Then they started encrypting the production servers to ransom them.

“Every backup job had succeeded for three years,” Li said. “The failure was that the thing meant to save them could be reached with the same keys as the thing being attacked.”

Have a story about someone leaving a gaping hole in their network? Share it with us at pwned@sitpub.com. Anonymity is available upon request.

The results were devastating. With the MES and SAP data both gone, the plant couldn't schedule production, ship parts, or track them.

By the time Li was called in, the plant had already resolved its ransomware situation. We don’t know whether it paid or not. The manufacturer did ask Li and his company to make sure that this couldn’t happen again.

Li’s fix was not radical, but it was instructive. He made sure that the company now keeps its backup system outside the production domain, uses unique credentials, and requires MFA to reduce the risk of unauthorized access even if a password is compromised. He also made sure that the company keeps at least one offline backup and runs a test restore quarterly to ensure it’s prepared for emergencies.®

‘The time to prepare for a cryptographic breakthrough is before it happens’: Zcash team urges faster deprecation of legacy shielded pool

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

About 400,000 ZEC remains in the Sapling pool, worth about $291 million at current prices.

Zcash Zcash (ZEC) infrastructure team Zakura urged the network to accelerate the deprecation of its legacy Sapling shielded pool, citing speculation of mathematical breakthroughs at AI labs.

In a post early Thursday, Zakura recommended users migrate any ZEC still in Sapling to Ironwood. These shielded pools allow users to hold and transfer ZEC without publicly revealing transaction amounts or sender and recipient addresses.

"The time to prepare for a cryptographic breakthrough is before it happens," the team wrote.

While practical breaking of the pairing-based cryptography used by Sapling remains highly speculative, Zakura also recommended a network upgrade to disable payments into Sapling. Roughly 400,000 ZEC, or approximately 2% of supply, remains in the pool, according to Zakura, worth roughly $291 million at current prices. Zakura said most wallets support both Sapling and Ironwood, and the impact should be minimal, with miners accounting for much of the activity left in Sapling.

"Less than 2.6% of all transactions in the last 30 days use Sapling, and of those, 66% are known miner flows, and it's highly likely most of the remainder are miners as well," the team said.

An earlier Zcash forum proposal in April had suggested waiting a year after a community decision to make Sapling withdrawal-only. At the time, the proposal put its balance at 600,000 ZEC, down from 1.1 million at the start of 2024. Zakura did not suggest a new deadline in its Thursday post.

Zakura pointed to a reported proof of the Riemann hypothesis, which concerns the distribution of prime numbers, and unsubstantiated rumors that AI labs had also made advances in integer factorization, the process of splitting large numbers into their prime factors. The difficulty of that task underpins RSA encryption, which is rarely used in cryptocurrencies, according to the team. Zakura said some math can be used for attacks on both RSA and pairing-based cryptography, so advances in one could help with the other, but the reported Riemann proof and the rumors do not show that either can be broken in practice.

"Practical pairing breaks are highly speculative," Zakura said, adding that transparent transactions and Ironwood do not rely on factoring or pairings.

Nevertheless, a hypothetical break in Sapling's pairing-based proof system would allow forged proofs, Zakura said, putting the funds in the pool at risk. The same cryptography does not secure users' private keys or their cryptographic records of shielded funds, so the team said developers could replace the proof system without replacing those records. However, Zcash would have to freeze Sapling before an attack compromises its state and users could then later recover their funds using a new ECDLP-based or hash-based SNARK, Zakura said.

Separately, the Ethereum Foundation doubled its protocol security team, The Block previously reported, arguing that AI is a "more urgent risk" than quantum computing and recommending Keccak-256 over four competing candidates for Ethereum's planned shift to hash-based cryptography. Zakura said it is also working on a proposal to add hash-based, post-quantum signatures for Zcash transparent transactions using SPHINCS+ with 192-bit security.

UHSAA associate director Jeff Cluff, 49, dies from complications from stroke

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jeff Cluff, who spent the past 12 years as a director with the UHSAA and was a champion of the mission of high school athletics, passed away at t. George Regional Hospital late Wednesday evening surrounded by family after complications from a stroke. He was 49.

Cluff suffered a stroke last year Thursday, and after the family updated his condition on social media Saturday, they received an outpouring of love and support from the thousands and thousands of people in the athletics community who Cluff impacted.

UHSAA associate director Jeff Cluff presents the state championship trophy to Corner Canyon after winning the 6A girls volleyball state championship against Skyridge at the UCCU Center at Utah Valley University in Orem on Thursday, Oct. 30, 2025. | Tess Crowley, Deseret News

Cluff joined the UHSAA in 2014 as the supervisor of officials. During his time with the UHSAA, he's also served as the director of baseball, along with overseeing compliance and eligibility, media services, arbiter sports, website administration, ticketing operations and numerous other roles. Prior to that he worked 13 years in the Dixie State University (now utah Tech) athletics department.

Here's the official family statement through social media on Thursday morning.

"It is with profound sadness that we announce the death of our beloved husband, father, brother, son and friend, Jeff cluff, 49, following complications from a stroke. Jeff died late Wednesday evening surrounded by loved ones.

Born in Tooele, Utah, jeff built a life centered on faith, family, friendship and service to others. He had a remarkable ability to make everyone he encountered fell like the most important person in the room. He was genuine, fiercely loyal, quick-witted and unapologetically himself. He told you what you needed to hear, stood by you when it mattered most and was always looking for ways to help others. These were values he and Monica worked to instill in their children, because raising a good family was his life's mission.

Whether on a the baseball field, among friends or at home with his family, Jeff lived with integrity, kindness and a deep sense of fairness. Those who knew him as an umpire knew the same man we knew at home: honest, consistent, firm in his convictions and kind at heart.

Jeff's generosity extended far beyond those closest to him, and in keeping with his lifelong commitment to helping others, Jeff's final act of generosity was to give the gift of life through organ donation, hopefully blessing the lives of others as he did ours.

While our hearts are broken, we find comfort in our faith, the memories we share adn the extraordinary outpouring of love from the community. Hearing the countless stories of how Jeff touched the lives of others has been a tremendous source of strength for our family.

The greatest tribute anyone could offer Jeff is to carry forward the qualities that defined him: show up for others, be genuine, find the good in people and make those around you feel valued.

We are deeply grateful for your continued prayers, love and support. We respectfully ask for our privacy as our family grieves and navigates the days ahead.

Monica, Kennedy (KP), Parker, Addison, Wrigs and Grady Cluff."

Cluff suffered a stroke last Thursday, and after tests doctors discovered a clot that was surgically removed over the weekend. Later that weekend doctors performed surgery to remove part of his skill to allow room for swelling and improve his chances of healing.

The family posted on social media on Monday, however, that the blood clot had returned and Cluff was bleeding in the brain. The family said there was nothing more doctors could do as blood thinners could cause further damage, and they were focused on making him comfortable while organ donation could be finalized.

Cluff's been a fixture at high school state tournaments across the state for over a decade, championing the UHSAA's message that student participation in activities is a vital educational tool to help youth develop essential life skills.

Earlier this week, former UHSAA colleague and current Crimson Cliffs principal Kim Monkres told the Deseret News, "It's such a loss. He's like a brother."

"He just genuinely loved people and took care of everyone. Always asking how you were, how can I help you. Those in the officials world know he loved them and cared for them, and genuinely was their biggest champion and wanted them to have a great experience and to love their jobs and take pride in it."

Monkres added the outpouring of love and support directed toward Cluff and his family has been, "phenomenal and just speaks to his character and how genuine he was and how much people loved him."

A GoFundMe page has been set up by a family member to help the family cover medical and funeral expenses for Jeff Cluff.

Vitalik Buterin Is Designing Chips: Could Ethereum Become a Hardware Company and Overtake Nvidia?

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Vitalik Buterin is taking Ethereum’s decentralization philosophy down to the silicon level. The Ethereum co-founder published a deep dive into open-source chip design today, covering everything from transistor architecture and manufacturing to hardware security. His central argument? It could have implications far beyond Ethereum, and computing hardware shouldn’t remain a proprietary black box.

Could Ethereum eventually move beyond blockchain infrastructure and build its own hardware ecosystem? Vitalik’s latest exploration makes that prospect an interesting thought experiment, particularly as Nvidia dominates the computing industry.

Vitalik’s argument starts with a familiar problem in crypto: trust. Software can be audited, code can be published, and blockchain transactions can be independently verified. Hardware, however, remains considerably harder to inspect.

A chip can contain hidden vulnerabilities or malicious modifications that users cannot easily detect. Even a secure wallet application cannot guarantee safety if the hardware underneath it has been compromised. This has happened recently with Ledger, and Ledger is not the only one, with Trezor and other hardware wallets being recently compromised.

“Not your silicon, not your keys.” – Vitalik put the problem bluntly.

The phrase extends crypto’s self-custody mantra into the physical computing layer. Owning private keys might not mean much if users cannot trust the hardware handling them.

This is why Vitalik argues that “the hardware layer must be open for participation and tinkering too.” He wants hardware designs to become accessible to developers, researchers, and independent auditors, allowing them to examine and modify components rather than blindly trusting manufacturers.

This could eventually create opportunities for Ethereum hardware focused on security, cryptographic computing, and decentralized infrastructure. Ethereum validators, hardware wallets, and zero-knowledge proof systems all depend on computing components that users need to trust.

Nvidia built its dominance by turning GPUs into the backbone of artificial intelligence. Its hardware, software ecosystem, and CUDA platform have made competition increasingly difficult.

But Vitalik’s vision displays a different approach. Instead of chasing the fastest processors, Ethereum hardware could prioritize openness, verifiability, and security.

Vitalik acknowledges that open-source hardware faces performance limitations. Advanced chip manufacturing remains expensive and tightly controlled, leaving open designs behind the industry’s most sophisticated commercial processors.

Yet that trade-off might not matter for every workload. Vitalik writes that “Secure hardware may be 10x less efficient or more” while still being sufficient for many computing needs. That’s a striking contrast to an industry obsessed with squeezing more performance from increasingly expensive chips.

Specialized silicon designed for cryptographic operations, privacy-preserving computing, secure data processing, or decentralized networks could benefit from open designs. Developers could modify the architecture, researchers could inspect the circuitry, and manufacturers could compete to produce chips from publicly available specifications.

Ethereum already has an extensive developer community experimenting with cryptography and distributed computing. Extending that culture into silicon could give the ecosystem a foothold in hardware without immediately challenging Nvidia’s AI infrastructure.

The ambition could be less about building another GPU giant and more about creating a competing hardware philosophy.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Vitalik’s argument reaches beyond blockchain. He sees open hardware as a broader computing movement, where users can inspect, verify, and modify the machines they depend on.

That could eventually extend into personal computers, servers, IoT devices, and secure computing infrastructure. Hardware designed around transparency could become valuable as AI, financial services, and sensitive personal data move into increasingly powerful machines.

Nvidia’s dominance isn’t going to disappear overnight. But Ethereum has never needed to follow the traditional technology industry’s playbook.

Its greatest strength has been building infrastructure around open participation rather than centralized ownership. If that philosophy eventually reaches the silicon powering modern computing, Ethereum could find itself competing in an industry few would have expected.

Vitalik’s latest deep dive into chip design raises a question: What happens when crypto’s decentralization movement stops at software and starts building the hardware, too?

Semiconductor chips are the new oil. Here's why

📅 2026-11-26  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The artificial intelligence boom has pushed semiconductors past oil and gas in global export value for the first time, according to a Deutsche Bank analysis of International Trade Centre data.

Yet the milestone arrives at an awkward moment, just as investors reassess the outlook for artificial intelligence spending and drive down the price of semiconductor stocks. Last week, before Monday's breathtaking reversal, semiconductor stocks suffered their worst weekly loss in a quarter century, with the PHLX Semiconductor Index (SOX) plunging nearly 11% last Friday alone, capping a more than 17% weekly decline.

Nvidia soared more than 31% Monday after the leading maker of artificial intelligence chips had fallen for a seventh straight week, plunging 46% during that stretch. Then it fell again, dropping almost 10% on Tuesday and Wednesday. Advanced Micro Devices tumbled 20% in two days last Thursday and Friday. Micron Technology sank 27% over the seven days ended last Friday.

"For decades we've talked about the petrodollar. Perhaps increasingly we need to think about the chip dollar," Jim Reid, global head of macro and thematic research at Deutsche Bank wrote in a report this month.

Semiconductor exports overtook oil and gas for the first time during the January-March quarter of 2026, reaching about $1.61 trillion, compared with $1.53 trillion for oil and gas, the report said, using four-quarter rolling totals.

The gap widened in the second quarter, when semiconductor exports totaled about $1.60 trillion and oil and gas exports reached about $1.47 trillion. Disruptions to oil supplies through the Strait of Hormuz after the Iran war helped widen the gap. Daily oil flows through the Strait collapsed nearly 77%, to 4.9 million barrels in the second quarter of 2026 from 21.6 million barrels in the fourth quarter of 2025, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

Yet the demand for semiconductor has proven insatiable in the face of soaring data center construction, boosting global trade in the increasingly scarce AI component. Hundreds of billions of dollars of orders for graphics processing units and data centers aim at providing AI computing power, Rothschild & Co. Redburn wrote in a report last Thursday.

"Compute will be the new commodity fueling this transformation on a global scale," Rothschild analyst Cara Thomson said.

The shift toward chips has macroeconomic implications just as the explosion in oil trade did in the 1970s.

Oil-exporting countries historically recycled much of their surpluses into the purchase of U.S. Treasuries and other bonds, helping finance U.S. deficits and underpin demand for other government debt.

Reid at Deutsche Bank said that since semiconductor-surplus countries in Asia are more closely tied to U.S. tech companies, some chip-export earnings could flow back into U.S. technology stocks, startups and data centers, further helping to finance the AI investment cycle.

The changing dynamics in global trade could also tilt the mix of global financial flows, Reid said. "So welcome to the chip-dollar era: potentially more growth, more excitement, more volatility, and structurally higher yields" in corporate and government debt.

US defense officials considered using Cold War law to force Nvidia to reinstate Jensen Huang as CEO following his resignation, report claims

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Defense Production Act is a 1950 law that allows a president to intervene in the economy and is usually used to prompt companies to supply the military during wartime.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Following the resignation of Jensen Huang as Nvidia's CEO after admitting to an affair with a suspected Chinese spy, U.S. defense leaders considered invoking a Cold War-era law in an attempt to force the company to reinstate him, according to a new report. Huang was given his CEO job back earlier this week, with the board reminding the founder of the importance of "candor and honesty" in the future. Now, The Washington Post reports that U.S. officials were so keen to ensure that Huang was reinstated that they brainstormed ideas as to how they could make it happen. One of the options on the table was the Defense Production Act, or DFA, a 1950 law that allows presidents to interfere with private companies. The law is usually used to force those companies to supply the military during wartime, but Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth and his senior deputy, Steve Feinberg, wondered whether it could also be used to put pressure on Nvidia's board.

While the DFA doesn't explicitly give the president the power to place a particular person into the CEO role at a company, it could still be wielded to achieve the same result. “Essentially, they could use the DPA to force Nvidia to shift more of its production to military projects,” one of three people reportedly familiar with the discussions said. Such a move would have limited Nvidia's ability to sell components for a profit, affecting its production plans and share price as a result.

The way in which U.S. officials pushed for Huang's return before his November 23 reinstatement is unprecedented. Current U.S. President Donald Trump called for Huang to be given his job back on multiple occasions, including when talking to reporters at the White House. Trump also confirmed that he called into an Nvidia board meeting to pressure members to bring Huang back just 11 days after he resigned. And with Huang still set to face questions from China congressional and Senate Banking committees, Trump has admitted that a pardon for the CEO is "always an option." This came after it was suggested that Huang could plead the Fifth Amendment for fear of incriminating himself and landing himself in legal hot water.

The U.S. military views Nvidia and its advanced AI chips as vital to national security, perhaps explaining why officials have been so keen to ensure Huang's return. Trump and Huang have a strong relationship, with the former joining the president aboard Air Force One for a recent trip to China. This came after Huang was initially snubbed for a trip that also included former Apple CEO Tim Cook and SpaceX CEO Elon Musk.

Is Micron Stock a Buy After HPE Said Memory Will Stay Scarce Into 2028?

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A big memory buyer and a bearish bank are now at least a year apart on when the shortage ends.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 26, 2026 at 8:17AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Micron Technology ›

Hewlett Packard Enterprise (HPE +1.57%) told investors on Nov. 23 that it still can't get all the memory chips it wants. And it doesn't think that'll change soon.

"We expect supply of memory, NAND, and many other critical components to remain constrained to at least the second half of calendar 2028," CEO Antonio Neri said on the company's earnings call that evening.

That seems like good news for Micron Technology (MU -4.53%), which makes DRAM (the memory computers use while they work) and NAND flash storage chips. But the memory maker's shares slid anyway. Micron closed at $707.70 on Nov. 23, just before the call. As of this writing on Nov. 25, the stock traded near $550, less than half the $1,213.56 it closed at on June 25, its record.

Meanwhile, BNP Paribas downgraded the shares, saying memory supply might catch up to demand a year sooner than it had thought.

The tech firm said in March that DRAM and NAND make up over half the cost of the parts in a traditional server. And its inventory more than doubled in fiscal 2026, from around $6.4 billion on Oct. 31, 2025, to $11.8 billion by July 31 and $14.1 billion by Oct. 31, 2026.

HPE's timeline seems to have stretched, too. In March, Neri said he expected high DRAM and NAND prices to "persist well into 2027."

CFO Marie Myers said the $14.1 billion inventory reflected higher costs for commodity parts like memory, purchases for customer orders, and what she called "strategic buys." She added that HPE's commodity costs climbed by significant double-digit percentages in 2026, and she expects smaller increases ahead. But smaller increases are still increases, not the price drops a glut would cause.

BNP Paribas thinks one could. In a note reported on Nov. 24, it cut its rating on Micron to neutral from outperform. It now says DRAM and NAND supply and demand might approach balance starting in mid-2027 and drive average selling prices down. It flagged risks to big cloud companies' data center plans for 2028 and new factory capacity coming online.

Micron's stock dropped 11% that day, and again on Nov. 25, when research firm TrendForce said NAND flash might move into oversupply in 2027. TrendForce still expects DRAM to stay tight through 2027, and DRAM made up about three-quarters of Micron's revenue in fiscal 2026.

Part of the new factory capacity BNP mentioned is Micron's own. Its first new fab in Idaho should start making wafers in mid-2027, and a facility in Taiwan should begin meaningful shipments around then too. But Micron says output at a new fab ramps up over a few quarters. And its other planned fabs, including a second one in Idaho and a new NAND factory in Singapore, aren't set to start output until the second half of 2028 or later.

"[W]e do not have line of sight to when supply and demand will return to balance," management wrote about DRAM in Micron's prepared remarks for its Sept. 30 earnings call.

HPE's 2028 date matches Micron's building schedule. BNP's earlier date seems to rely more on demand weakening.

Micron's fiscal 2028 ends on Aug. 31, 2028, so HPE's outlook covers nearly all of the year. If the outlook holds, Micron could spend most of fiscal 2028 selling into a market still short on chips. If BNP is right, prices could be dropping for most of it.

The stock price looks closer to the second case.

At roughly $550, Micron trades at a price-to-earnings ratio of about 7.4 on its fiscal 2026 results. Based on the average analyst estimate for fiscal 2028, the price is below 3 times earnings.

Even BNP sees Micron's earnings per share bottoming at $86 in 2029, over the $74.33 per share Micron earned in fiscal 2026 under generally accepted accounting principles (GAAP). The stock looks cheap even on that bearish estimate.

One buyer's view could be wrong, of course. But I think HPE's outlook undercuts the glut worries, especially for DRAM, Micron's largest business. A buyer still stocking up at higher prices is tough to square with a stock priced for falling memory prices by mid-2027.

I'd consider buying some Micron shares here, in a position small enough to ride out more days like Nov. 24. If HPE starts pulling that 2028 date forward, like BNP just did with its own, I'd rethink that.
