India Targets at Least 10 Million AI Workers Through National AI Talent Mission, WAVES Film Bazaar Panel Hears

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

India plans to bring at least 10 million people into its AI workforce through the National AI Talent Mission, recently launched by Prime Minister Narendra Modi, according to Lt. Col. Vikram Singh Bhati, director of the National Council for Vocational Education and Training (NCVET).

Bhati was speaking at “AI and the Future of Creative Skills: Transforming India’s Media & Entertainment Workforce,” a panel at WAVES Film Bazaar, the market held alongside the 57th International Film Festival of India (IFFI) in Goa. Moderated by voice-over artist and actor Vijay Vikram Singh, the session also featured Amit Meena, director at the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship; K. Satish Nambudiripad, former director general of public broadcaster Doordarshan and chairperson of the Centre for Creative Economy and Public Policy at the National Institute of Technology Calicut; and Biren Ghose, founder and CEO of Astra Studios. Related Stories Jeff Goldblum, Marion Cotillard and John Turturro Dine With Morocco’s HRH Prince Moulay Rachid at Starry Royal Gala During Marrakech Film Festival 'Fjord' Director Cristian Mungiu Says It's Up to Audiences to Make Up Their Own Minds About Polarizing Palme d'Or Winner: 'The Film Is Not Divisive. Society Is Divided'

“It started with the ministry launching a scheme called SOAR (Skilling for AI Readiness),” Bhati said of the government’s push to prepare workers for AI. He also called on companies to take part, saying “the industry should start absorbing people in the apprenticeship model” to help existing workers and new entrants adapt.

AI is changing the production chain, from conceptualization to release, Meena said. “It is democratizing the whole process,” he said, stressing the need to reskill existing workers.

Nambudiripad warned that AI tools “tend to homogenize,” and that “India’s folklore, regional languages and cultural nuances could be flattened” if training did not account for the country’s diversity. “You ask five people to write a concept note… if you are going through AI, every one of them will write the same,” he said. He called for technical institutions, film schools and industry to work together, and for skilling to reach India’s smaller tier-two and tier-three cities.

Creative skills are shifting “from execution to imagination,” Ghose said, and from software proficiency to technology orchestration. “In the past we were wondering when the tools would catch up with us, and now we’re wondering when we would catch up with the tools,” he said. He also called for creative technology skills to be recognized in sectors beyond film and gaming.

“No technology has ever invented anything,” Ghose said. “Everything that got invented has been invented by the ideas of man.”

Chalaris: "Olympiacos in ottima forma fisica, in casa mette gli avversari sotto una pressione tremenda"

The final trailer for Avengers: Doomsday confirmed Steve Rogers's choice to go back in time to live with Peggy Carter in Avengers: Endgame kickstarts the plot of the new movie.

But, there's one timeline question I can't figure out: what does that mean for Old Steve Rogers? At the end of Endgame, Sam Wilson is given the shield by an elderly Steve, who grew old in the past with Peggy. So, if he actually had to return to the present to help fight Doctor Doom, shouldn't the old Steve have already lived through Doomsday?

It's a debate Marvel fans are having in the wake of the trailer, and nobody seems to agree.

Sooooo Do we think this Steve went through the events of Doomsday or?November 22, 2026

"I keep going back and forth about this but probably? The only way for Doomsday Steve to have any stakes is if it's the same one, rather than a new branch variant created by Doom's disruption," thinks one Marvel fan.

"When Loki comes back and talks to him in the past, he created another timeline. The old Steve Rogers is a different Steve from the Doomsday, although they had the same starting point," says someone else.

"I'd be a little disappointed if he didn't. Seems like there's a fantastic opportunity to make this hit harder upon a rewatch. I personally wouldn't pass up the opportunity. It even goes as far to present that Sam Wilson survives and leads the new Avengers proudly," thinks another fan.

"My guess is No. Since Endgame explained that once something happened, it happened, and any change in the past will only create another timeline – meaning this old Steve gave Sam the shield, and only later they met in Doomsday – then this old Steve is but a variant of Doomsday Steve," is another person's theory.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

"Ig yes he went through all the events of Doomsday and Secret Wars yet he remained mute because he knew you can't change the future as it will branch a new timeline so he calmly accepted the truth and relaxed," speculates another person.

That's not the only timeline question, either, since it looks like Steve and Peggy actually live across two different timelines in the Marvel Phase 6 movie – one where they're visited by Loki, and one where the Fantastic Four arrive to talk to them.

We don't have long to find out what's actually going on. Doomsday arrives this December 18, and it pits Earth's Mightiest Heroes against Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom.

What channel is Hearts v Monaco? TV, live stream and highlights for Conference League clash

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This article contains affiliate links. We may earn a small commission on items purchased through this article, but that does not affect our editorial judgement.

Hearts will attempt to claim a major European scalp when they host French giants Monaco in the Uefa Conference League on Thursday evening.

Wouter Vrancken’s side go into the match as underdogs but are in need of a positive result after collecting just one point from their opening three league phase fixtures to sit 32nd in the Conference League table.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

A 1-1 draw against Norwegian side Nordsjælland at Tynecastle was followed by away defeats at Trabzonspor (2-0) and FC Thun (3-1) to leave the Tynecastle club facing an uphill task to qualify for the knockout stages.

Teams typically need seven or eight points from their six league phase matches to finish inside the top 24 and reach the knockout play-off round. Eight points virtually guarantees progression, while seven points usually puts a club right on the cut-off line where tie-breakers decide who advances.

Hearts know this only too well, having failed to progress on goals scored after collecting seven points to finish 25th during their 2024-25 Conference League campaign.

Monaco arrive in Edinburgh fourth in the table with a maximum haul of nine points following victories over CSKA Sofia, Nordsjælland and Freiburg. Their domestic form has been less impressive of late with Filipe Luís’ side currently on a five-game winless run on Ligue 1, where they are eighth in the standings.

The Conference League match between Hearts and Monaco takes place at Tynecastle Park, Edinburgh, on Thursday, November 26, 2026. Kick-off is 5.45pm.

The match will be shown live on TNT Sports 3 with coverage starting at 5pm.

Fans can stream the match live via HBO Max or Amazon Prime Video with a TNT Sports subscription.

Free highlights will be posted on the TNT Sports Football YouTube channel shortly after full-time.

Hearts welcome midfielder Blair Spittal back into their Europa League squad following his recovery from an ankle injury. Malachi Fagan-Walcott drops out with a groin problem. Two players who featured in the 8-0 win over Dundee on Saturday, Eduardo Ageu and Leart Kabashi, cannot play as they are not registered in the Europa League squad list.

Midfielder Tomas Magnusson is still completing concussion protocol before he can be declared available. Defender Craig Halkett and the versatile Oisin McEntee were back on the Riccarton training pitch on Wednesday, although they will not be back until next month. Winger Calvin Miller has also undergone surgery on his knee and will be out for between six weeks and two months.

Stephen Kingsley (hamstring), Harry Milne (knee) and Tom Renaud (ankle) are also unavailable.

Monaco are without five players. Goalkeeper Philippe Kohn, defender Mohammed Salisu, midfielders Stanis Idumbo and Aleksandr Golovin, plus forward Paris Brunner were all left in the Principality for treatment.

An all-German officiating team will take charge match. Daniel Schlager is the on-field referee and will be supported on the touchlines by assistant referees Sven Waschitzki and Tobias Fritsch. Robin Braun has been appointed as the fourth official. In the video operations room, Benjamin Cortus will serve as the Video Assistant Referee (VAR), alongside Nicolas Winter as the Assistant VAR (AVAR).

Hearts 11/2, Draw 18/5, Monaco 8/15 - via oddschecker.com.

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For Ece Temelkuran, the human condition is "unhomed". Speaking to FRANCE 24, the Turkish author cites the rise of artificial intelligence, climate change and the authoritarian drift as reasons why one may no longer recognise the place one calls home. This feeling of "spiritual homelessness" is something she explores in her latest book, "Nation of Strangers: Rebuilding Home in the 21st Century". Most of all, she highlights the importance of human connection: "In survival mode (...) you live on the kindness of others."

"Here We Go Again" - After Delaying For Silksong, Indie Dev Braces For A Sea Of Sorrow Clash

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There were a good few indies set for launch in September last year that ended up being delayed to escape the blast radius of Hollow Knight: Silksong. Aeternum Game Studios' metroidvania, Aeterna Lucis, was one such title, pushing its release date to 3rd December 2026 to ensure it was way out of the way of whatever Team Cherry was cooking up. Heh, well...

Yesterday, the Silksong dev announced an upcoming 'Cherry Time' special presentation, promising news and announcements from the studio, and an update on the hotly-anticipated Sea of Sorrow DLC. The showcase is tomorrow (27th November), so there's every chance that, in dodging the initial launch, Aeterna Lucis has wound up right in the way of the DLC.

This time, however, the dev is standing firm. "A lot of people have been waiting for the Queen of Light for a long time now, and we don't want to make them wait any longer," the dev wrote in a message on social media, neatly captioned "Here we go again".

It's all very jokey and light-hearted, with Aeternum even proposing a Silksong bundle if the dates do happen to clash. Still, there's room for a little cursing of fate: "With all the love in the world: is it just us, or does the universe have it in for us?"

You'll find the full statement below, followed by the post from Bluesky:

A little over a year ago, just as we were about to launch our game, you announced Silksong. We stepped aside, pushed the whole project back a year and, honestly, it was one of the best decisions we've ever made: the genre had an incredible year, and we got to enjoy Silksong as fans and polish Aeterna Lucis into the game we always dreamed it would be.

Now here we are again, just days from launch. Aeterna Lucis, our new metroidvania, comes out on December 3... and you announce Cherry Time. This time we're staying put. Not to go against you, but because a lot of people have been waiting for the Queen of Light for a long time, and we don't want to make them wait any longer.

Whatever happens on Friday, we'll be watching like any other fan. And if Sea of Sorrow ends up launching on the same day, the real winners will be metroidvania fans.

That said, we have to ask, with all the love in the world: is it just us, or does the universe have it in for us? Hahaha. And since you're about to wreck our lives all over again, the least we can do is make a bundle together.

With all our love (and a little bit of fear), The Aeternum Game Studios team

Official statement, part II. Here we go again…

While we can't imagine Sea of Sorrow will have quite the industry-shaking impact that the base game did, it sure would be unfortunate to see an indie like Aeterna Lucis caught in the line of fire again. Hey, at least the dev is able to have a laugh with it.

Some excellent running shoes are going for a song

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Black Friday sales are always a great to restock your running shoe wardrobe, because there are incredible deals available on sneakers from pretty much every brand.

Amazon's Black Friday running shoe deals are especially good on Asics, Brooks and Skechers, while most brands running their own individual sales too, with Nike and Saucony offering standout savings in particular.

I've picked out the best deals on running shoes I've tested myself below, with one that caught my eye in particular being the $50 saving on the Adidas Hyperboost Edge available at Amazon.

The Aero Razor is a lightweight daily trainer that comes alive in interval workouts but also feels comfortable at easy paces. For me, it matches up well with very popular shoes like the Evo SL, but is less well-known, meaning you can get it for the silly price of $63 in the sales right now.

While $150 is still a lot to spend on a running shoe, the Adidas Hyperboost Edge was one of the biggest and most exciting launches of 2026, and a 25% discount is not to be sniffed at. It's a very versatile max-cushioned shoe and while the style will split opinion, I think it's eye-catching in the right way.

The Nike Pegasus 42 is the latest model of Nike's popular workhorse trainer. It's a good option for walking and gym workouts alongside running thanks to its durable, stable design. You can get this price, which is the cheapest I've seen the Pegasus 42, by picking the color of the shoe reduced to $95 and then using the code 'BFRIDAY' at checkout.

The Puma Velocity Nitro 4 is similar to the Nike Pegasus as a high-drop daily trainer with a long-lasting, comfortable design. It's a bit lighter than the Pegasus though, and I rate it more highly as a good value cushioned shoe that always appears in sales.

Saucony's sale allows you to stack discounts by adding a reduced shoe to your cart and then using the code 'CHEER' at checkout for a further 25% off. That means you can get the Saucony Endorphin Speed 5 for just $82, which is an amazing price for an impressive plated trainer like this.

The Nike Structure Plus is the most comfortable stability shoe in Nike's line-up, and stacking discounts allows you to get it for well under $100 in the Nike sale. Pick the color reduced to $104 in the sale, then use the code 'BFRIDAY' to take another 25% off at checkout.

The Adidas Adizero Evo SL is a long term favorite of mine because of its fun, fast ride. The price of the Evo SL varies with the size and color you pick on Amazon, with the cheapest I've seen it being $100 for the white and red shoe. If you want a more reliable discount, Adidas has the Evo SL for $120 in its sale.

The Hoka Clifton 11 is about as minor an update as running shoe updates get, so I'd definitely get the Hoka Clifton 10 instead in the Hoka sale. This deal reduces the Clifton 10 to the lowest price I've yet seen it. It's very comfortable for both walking and running, and a variety of colors are on sale.

The Asics Gel-Kayano 32 is a supportive stability shoe with a lot a comfortable foam in its midsole. With the Asics Gel-Kayano 33 launching recently, the previous model is available in a few sales, with this deal for $124 on Amazon being the price to beat.

While the Hoka Challenger 8 is not as well known as the Bondi and Clifton, it might be a better option than both of them for many runners. It's a comfortable and versatile all-terrain shoe that's worth looking at if you mix up the surfaces you run on.

The Asics Superblast 3 impressed me when I tested it this year, with a softer, bouncier feel than the Superblast 2. However, the Superblast 2 is still better than most other new shoes I tried in 2026, and it's $50 cheaper than its successor. It's a highly cushioned daily trainer that can handle everything from easy runs to interval workouts.

What you'll get:📈 A VPN for life – no questions🔐 Verified no-logs policy📱 Lifetime password manager✨ 10 simultaneous connections

FastestVPN’s lifetime plan, which includes its no-frills VPN and password manager, now costs just $27 upfront. Use the code FUTUREBF2026 to claim the discount. As mentioned earlier, this is the cheapest VPN I'd actually recommend buying.

Of course, FastestVPN is nowhere near the best VPNs, such as NordVPN, ExpressVPN, or Surfshark. But during our testing, we found that it gets the basics right. Starting with the all-important privacy, FastestVPN is headquartered in the privacy-friendly jurisdiction of Cayman Islands, which sits outside the Five Eyes, Nine Eyes, and Fourteen Eyes data-sharing alliances.

Combined with a strict no-logs policy that’s been independently audited, you can trust FastestVPN to keep your VPN session data private. There’s also a reliable kill switch, DNS leak protection, and support for modern VPN protocols like WireGuard, OpenVPN, and IKEv2.

FastestVPN isn't completely bereft of modern features, though, and privacy-conscious users will appreciate its specialty Double VPN and P2P servers, albeit with limited options. It might not be as feature-rich as the best password managers, but it covers all the basics – from generating and saving strong passwords to storing logins and sensitive records in a secure vault and syncing everything across devices.

Coca-Cola Is the Most Boring Stock I Own. After 64 Years of Dividend Raises, I'll Never Sell.

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's a blue chip dividend stock that you don't have to second-guess.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 26, 2026 at 7:39AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Coca-Cola ›

I consider myself adventurous, but I often prefer boring when it comes to the stocks I own. I have my fair share of growth stocks, for sure, but my staple "boring" stock is Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%).

Although Coca-Cola is up over 40% this year (as of Nov. 24), its main appeal has long been its dividend. It has increased its annual dividend for 64 consecutive years, making it one of the market's premier Dividend Kings ( companies with at least 50 consecutive years of dividend hikes). Considering that and its sustained success, it's a stock I'll never sell.

You can count on one hand the number of brands more well-known globally than Coca-Cola. Its products (especially its namesake soda) are available in virtually every part of the world, and its asset-light business model enables it to operate efficiently.

Usually, if a company wants distribution somewhere, it needs plants, warehouses, and delivery fleets in that market. In Coca-Cola's case, it relies on bottling and distribution partners to do so. Instead of owning factories and delivery fleets around the world, Coca-Cola sells concentrates and syrups to its partners and lets them do the heavy lifting.

Keeping its business asset-light lets Coca-Cola maintain high margins and an ultra-safe dividend. You don't get to 64 years of dividend increases without consistent, high-margin cash flows.

Not many companies fit the "recession-proof" narrative, but Coca-Cola is one of them. Even when the economy slows and money is tight, people still buy their favorite Coca-Cola brands. That's the beauty of its brand moat.

And despite being the undisputed leader in its space, Coca-Cola hasn't gotten complacent with its drink portfolio. It constantly refines its lineup to adjust to changing consumer preferences. Just six years ago, it had over 400 brands in its portfolio. Today, it has around half of that.

When you have an option for virtually every occasion, you keep customers in your ecosystem. It's how the company has sustained success for so long.

Coca-Cola is a premium business that's priced like one. At the time of this writing, it's trading at 28.9 times its earnings, which is higher than its average over the past few years and higher than the S&P 500's (^GSPC -0.25%)23.7 P/E ratio. The stock is still a buy, but after a 40% run-up this year, the upside may be more limited. Luckily, that doesn't affect its dividend.

Coca-Cola is always a good long-term investment, but with bear-market fears lingering, a short-term silver lining is that the stock typically outperforms the market during sell-offs. Past results don't guarantee future performance, but Coca-Cola has strong momentum at a time when the tech sector is dragging down the broader market.

A Mark Cuban-backed AI startup is helping families turn Thanksgiving conversations with their elderly relatives into lasting memories

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As 81.5 million Americans travel at least 50 miles from home for Thanksgiving, conversations around the dinner table could present a rare opportunity for people to learn more about their aging loved ones. A new class of AI startups wants to help them preserve those conversations forever.

Charlie Greene, the cofounder and CEO of Remento, first learned about the importance of recording memories when his father, Don Greene, died during the hijacking of United Airlines Flight 93 on 9/11.

A 10-year-old boy at the time of the terrorist attack, which resulted in his father’s plane crashing in Shanksville, Penn., the now 34-year-old Greene mostly has a collection of old home videos to remember him by.

So when his mother Claudette, 74, was diagnosed with stage-three lung cancer, he quickly acted to record her memories. With the intention of conducting an oral history interview, Greene searched “questions to ask a parent,” which Google morbidly autocompleted with “before they die.”

When he started asking her the questions like “How did you get to elementary school as a kid,” she lit up, surprised he was interested at all.

“The thing that blew me away about that experience was how unmorbid it felt,” he said.

Claudette is in remission, but her battle with cancer still left Greene “unhealthily fixated” on the idea of how to best preserve loved ones’ memories. In October 2023, he launched Remento, which sends older “storytellers” a weekly text or email reminder prompting them to respond to photos or questions chosen in advance by their family members, such as, “What was the hardest thing you had to overcome as a child?”

After gathering a maximum of a year’s worth of responses, the AI converts the raw transcript into a naturally flowing narrative that goes into a physical book complete with images and QR codes linking the text to the original recordings.

Greene told Fortune Thanksgiving presents a great opportunity for people to discover new memories or learn new twists on well worn stories from their relatives.

“One of the things I want for my daughter is the chance to sit next to her grandmother and hear her say ‘Believe it or not that’s me.’ Thanksgiving is a pretty good excuse to pull an old album off the shelf and make room for the two of them on the couch,” he said.

Last year, Greene secured a $300,000 investment from billionaire Mark Cuban during an episode of Shark Tank in exchange for a 10% stake in his company. He has raised a total of $4.3 million since launch.

Greene said he understands some people’s skepticism of AI, because “A lot of what AI is proposing to the world doesn’t feel actually that good to our lives right now.”

Widespread anxiety about AI replacing human workers as well as concerns over its environmental footprint have contributed to some people’s increasing skepticism toward the technology. A March study of more than 3,000 people by the nonprofit Jobs For the Future found that 38% of respondents said AI was doing more harm than good, while less than half said the technology was a net positive.

Yet Greene hasn’t gotten as much pushback about the company’s use of AI as he had expected because Remento is using the technology to provide its users with something deeply personal, he added.

“Investors want to invest in AI, because it’s an exciting place to be. But I think what we’re experiencing is people have problems and they want them solved, and they want consumer technology to be able to solve those problems,” he said.

Kindred Tales, a rival platform cofounded by Nick Hern and his wife Rebecca Hern, has seen a similar trend with their customers. The company has both AI and non-AI versions of its product.

While the non-AI version requires users to type out their responses to weekly prompts, the AI version helps by providing speech-to-text transcription and some light editing. It can also write a first draft of the interviewee’s story, if they want.

Before 2025, just over half of the company’s customers chose the AI version, but that number jumped to 80% after 2025.

“Customers increasingly see AI as a meaningful part of the storytelling experience, not just an extra,” Nick Hern told Fortune.

Part of the reason is that the company’s AI chatbot, called “Ali,” gives the interview process a conversational feel by teasing more out of users with follow-up questions crafted to their responses.

AI has helped Kern, who also works full time for Thomson Reuters, run the company as well. He said he has used the technology to help filter emails, as well as slowly replace freelancers that he previously hired to help him craft marketing and social content.

At a time when companies are spending billions to bring AI to every American, Greene, said, despite its flaws, AI has allowed Remento’s customers’, as well as himself, to record moments with family members that are invaluable.

“AI in our company is being used to get people to do things that they’ve always wanted to do but haven’t been able to, and to make that experience as seamless and humanlike as possible,” he said.

A version of this story was published on Fortune.com on April 25, 2026.

Role: Reporter Marco Quiroz-Gutierrez is a reporter for Fortune covering general business news.

Topics Black Friday Gaming Pokemon Trading Cards

If you spot a solid discount on a Pokémon TCG pack, box, or collection you haven’t seen before (like a 30th Celebration ETB, Delta Reign Booster Bundle, etc.), it’s worth buying now rather than waiting for Black Friday.

When you see a particular price drop, that deal is more likely specific to one or several sellers than to the industry market price across all sellers — depending on their own stock allocations. When that stock is gone, it’s uncertain whether those sellers will have any more units coming in, or even at the same lower price point.

Although more Pokémon cards are being printed than ever in 2026, the Pokémon TCG still has the same issue that sees any readily available selection of packs, or boxes of packs, being sold for above MSRP: There is nowhere near enough supply to meet the demand.

As a result, prices for readily available cards are dictated by market price: estimated by factors like pull rates, the most valuable cards in an expansion, how many print runs an expansion has had, and their overall contribution to the meta.

Day-to-day deals vary between standard Pokémon TCG retailers like Amazon, Walmart, and Target — but the best Black Friday deals are likely to be from third-party sellers operating through those storefronts. There are a lot of sellers competing for sales with one another to offload their stock, and Black Friday is still the ideal season to undercut other Pokémon TCG merchants. How Much Is Jensen Huang Worth to Nvidia? The Stock Market Just Gave a Clear Answer

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang’s return sent Nvidia’s stock soaring.

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 26, 2026 at 7:34AM EST

Jensen Huang's saga just took another unexpected turn. Nvidia's (NVDA -3.91%) board unanimously voted to reinstate him as CEO, less than a week after explicitly saying it wouldn't. The message the board is sending is clear. Despite Huang's extramarital affairs, including with an alleged Chinese spy and with one of his subordinates, he is extremely valuable to the company. Just how valuable? The market's reaction to Huang's reinstatement gives us a clue.

Jensen Huang. Image source: Getty Images.

It's no secret that Wall Street loves founder-led businesses, but why is that? There are several reasons. Let's discuss two of them. First, founders, who often own a substantial portion of the company, have a strong incentive to maximize shareholder value. Hired CEOs do too, to some extent. That's why equity-based compensation exists.

However, founders tend to have more skin in the game. They wouldn't just lose their job if their corporation failed, unlike many hired CEOs. They would lose a substantial portion of their wealth. Second, founders often have a much deeper knowledge of the business they created, its characteristics, risks, competitive advantages, etc. That may give them an edge in leading the business to greater heights. According to some research, founder-led companies generally outperform broader equities.

So, Wall Street's faith isn't blind -- it is backed by data. That brings us back to Huang. He helped found Nvidia in 1993 and made it one of the most valuable companies in the world, and the corporation at the center of the ongoing artificial intelligence (AI) revolution. Huang is one of the longest-serving founder-CEOs in corporate America. That counts for a lot

That's why the stock soared about 31%, gaining nearly $1 trillion in a single session, after it was announced that he was coming back. It was the largest single-day market-cap gain in history.

Huang's return reduces the uncertainty tied to Nvidia's CEO search. Before the announcement, the market didn't know whether it would take six weeks or six months, and it was hard to reliably assign probability estimates to each outcome. The market abhors uncertainty, so the news had a major calming effect for many investors. That doesn't mean that Nvidia is out of trouble.

The company still faces major investigations tied to Huang's scandals, and the man is scheduled to testify before Congress soon. Still, with Huang at the helm -- and even accounting for ongoing regulatory probes -- the stock looks attractive. The first sign of that is Nvidia's financial results. In the third quarter of its fiscal year 2027, which ended on Oct. 25, Nvidia crushed expectations.

The company's revenue soared 103% year over year to $115.8 billion, with data center revenue rising 110% compared to the year-ago period. Nvidia's non-GAAP gross margin remains exceptional for a company of this size at 74.9%, versus 73.5% in the year-ago period. Nvidia's non-GAAP earnings per share jumped 111% year over year to $2.68.

The company's AI tailwind is not slowing down much, if at all. It expects $130 billion in revenue for its fiscal year 2027 fourth quarter at the midpoint, representing a year-over-year increase of about 91%. Sure, that's a lower year-over-year revenue growth rate than in its recent third quarter, and so is Nvidia's 2028 fiscal year guidance of 75% year-over-year revenue growth.

But investors should remember two things. First, Nvidia tends to post stronger-than-expected results. It has done so more often than not for the better part of three years. So, a predicted 91% year-over-year growth rate may turn into something above 100% -- in fact, the company's Q3 2027 guidance implied its revenue would increase by about 89.5% compared to the year-ago period.

Further, a decelerating growth rate may indicate tougher year-over-year comparisons rather than slowing demand for the company's products. Nvidia clearly still has a large market to tap into, and with Huang back at the helm, it is as well-positioned as ever to do so. And even after its 31% jump, the stock is down about 36% from the all-time high reached a little over a month ago. At current levels, Nvidia stock remains a buy.

Black Friday is here once again, and if you've been waiting for the prices to drop before buying your next telescope, smart telescope or binoculars — this could be the time to pull the trigger.

But as we all know by now, retailers use these sales as a sneaky way to inflate their prices before the sale starts, so in many instances, their "sale" price is actually the same as it was before they put the price up. So how can you be sure that the price you're seeing is truly a deal?

Well, we keep track of prices throughout the year, and use tools to check when they fluctuate. We know whether something is a genuine deal, and, crucially, whether the product is actually worth buying in the first place.

We've searched the web for the best genuine deals on the telescopes, smart telescopes and binoculars that we have actually tested ourselves, so you can be confident you're actually saving money and not getting played by capitalism.

Telescopes give you breathtaking views of the moon and planets.

A great beginner telescope that the whole family can enjoy. This 4.5-inch Newtonian reflector comes with a 20mm eyepiece for scanning across the sky and a 10mm eyepiece for detailed views of the moon or spotting Saturn's rings.

The EQ mount requires a bit of tinkering, but it's a useful skill to have once you've figured it out, as it makes tracking objects across the sky easier than an Alt-Az mount.

This model usually retails for $319.99, so a $40 saving on a telescope of this quality is pretty good.

The Celestron StarSense Explorer range is great for anyone who wants to start stargazing but doesn't yet have any knowledge of the night sky, and this model in particular is a good option if you want to invest in something you can grow with.

The setup is a straightforward Alt-Azimuth mount, and you can use the smartphone dock to navigate your way around the sky with the guidance of the StarSense app.

It has a 5.1-inch (130mm) aperture and comes with 25mm and 10mm eyepieces, giving you beautiful views of the moon, Saturn's rings, Jupiter's cloud bands and wide views of some deep-sky objects like the Andromeda Galaxy and the Orion Nebula.

The price tends to fluctuate throughout the year, although on average it sits between $420 and $450. However, it's been the full $499.95 since September, so this price is definitely worth taking advantage of.

Read our full Celestron StarSense Explorer DX 130AZ review

Although it's very close in aperture to the StarSense DX 130AZ we listed above, the differences in optical design and focal length offer a different observing experience.

This model is the better choice if you want higher-magnification observations of the Moon and planets, thanks to its 1,250mm focal length. It also has a much shorter optical tube, so it's more compact and easier to transport. The Schmidt-Cassegrain design is more complex to manufacture than a Newtonian reflector, hence the difference in price between the two.

It was $579.99 throughout most of the year, rising to $589.99 in August, which it has been ever since.

Smart telescopes will locate, track and stack images for you, making astrophotography easier than ever.

Granted, it's not the biggest saving ever, and it isn't listed specifically as a Black Friday deal, but you can save $35 on the ZWO Seestar S30 Pro at Amazon. This is the cheapest we've seen it since its release, and it typically stays at the $699 RRP.

It's an incredible way to get into astrophotography, even for complete beginners. It's an all-in-one system that automatically locates, tracks and stacks images of stunning deep-space objects for you. It's compact and portable, with a quick and easy setup and produces beautiful 4K images.

Another fantastic saving from Unistellar, and the Odyssey Pro doesn't have as much reach as the eVscope 2, meaning it works better on wider objects.

Vaonis describes the Vespera 3 as the "most affordable premium smart telescope on the market," and judging from its specs, we'd be inclined to agree.

It has a new apochromatic quadruplet design that eliminates chromatic aberration and field curvature across the entire frame, excellent clarity even under city skies, a massive 11 hours of battery life and built-in multi-night capture.

It has a 50mm aperture and 245mm focal length, with a native image resolution of 8.3MP, or up to 24MP with covalENS mosaic mode.

If you liked the sound of the Vespera 3 but wanted something a little more powerful, the Vespera Pro 2 is $500 off for Black Friday.

Big astronomy binoculars are more portable than a telescope, making them ideal for camping trips and spontaneous stargazing outings.

Europe’s Economic Sovereignty Is All Talk and Little Action

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This issue is preventing our website from loading properly. Please review the following troubleshooting tips or contact us at [email protected].

Analysis: Europe’s Economic Sovereignty Is All Talk and Little Action

Create an FP account to save articles to read later.

Downloadable PDFs are a benefit of an FP subscription.

Contact us at [email protected] to learn about upgrade options, unlocking the ability to gift this article.

Get audio access with any FP subscription.

European debates about economic security often sound like a succession of buzzwords: “strategic autonomy,” “de-risking,” “diversification.” For all the lofty aspirations, none of these policies have done much to change Europe’s trajectory. As the European Council on Foreign Relations’ new report on global interdependence (which I co-authored) documents, the European Union keeps growing more reliant on the United States in finance, technology, and trade.

Let’s start with finance. Nearly 25 years after the euro began circulating, one might assume that it is the go-to currency for European Union firms doing business outside the bloc. The data tells a different story: In 2024, EU firms used the euro for only 46 percent of their transactions outside the EU, down just 2 percentage points from 2016, compared to a steady 41 percent invoiced in dollars. Despite long-standing calls from the European Commission for companies to switch to euros, the dollar’s prominence partly reflects its dominance of energy markets; in 2024, EU firms used dollars for 87 percent of their petroleum imports.

European debates about economic security often sound like a succession of buzzwords: “strategic autonomy,” “de-risking,” “diversification.” For all the lofty aspirations, none of these policies have done much to change Europe’s trajectory. As the European Council on Foreign Relations’ new report on global interdependence (which I co-authored) documents, the European Union keeps growing more reliant on the United States in finance, technology, and trade.

Let’s start with finance. Nearly 25 years after the euro began circulating, one might assume that it is the go-to currency for European Union firms doing business outside the bloc. The data tells a different story: In 2024, EU firms used the euro for only 46 percent of their transactions outside the EU, down just 2 percentage points from 2016, compared to a steady 41 percent invoiced in dollars. Despite long-standing calls from the European Commission for companies to switch to euros, the dollar’s prominence partly reflects its dominance of energy markets; in 2024, EU firms used dollars for 87 percent of their petroleum imports.

Next, look at European financial investments. Since U.S. President Donald Trump embraced tariffs in 2025 and then threatened ttempted to seize Greenland in early 2026, there has been talk in some EU capitals that divesting Europe’s significant holdings of U.S. Treasurys could be a way to pressure Washington. EU-based investment funds and central banks must have missed the memo: Together, they made net purchases of U.S. Treasurys totaling $56 billion between January 2025 and this August. (The data excludes Belgium, Ireland, and Luxembourg, as these countries’ hefty holdings mostly reflect their role as financial hubs.)

Despite Paris’s role as Europe’s vocal champion of strategic autonomy, French investors accounted for 61 percent of these purchases—cementing France’s status as the top EU holder of U.S. Treasurys, with $363 billion in holdings as of August.

EU appetite for U.S. public debt stems from one simple fact: At just $7 trillion, the combined stock of German, French, and EU-issued sovereign bonds is five times smaller than the stock of U.S. Treasurys. Without a greater supply of euro-denominated safe assets, EU funds have little choice but to accumulate U.S. Treasurys. This increases Europe’s exposure to threats from the United States, such as the advice of former Federal Reserve Board of Governors member Stephen Miran to withhold part of the interest that Washington pays on foreign-owned Treasurys.

These days, all eyes are on artificial intelligence, and this sector highlights the extent to which calls for European de-risking are going unanswered in the tech field. In late October, Epoch AI ranked 64 models at or above the level of OpenAI’s GPT-5. Of these, 49 were American, 14 were Chinese, and just one was European—France’s Mistral Large 4. It’s no surprise, then, that American models are becoming the default choice for European firms. According to the German tech industry association Bitkom, 76 percent of German companies using AI rely on OpenAI’s ChatGPT, up from 70 percent last year. Not a single one of the surveyed firms uses Mistral.

U.S. dominance of Western AI is unlikely to end any time soon. Epoch researchers also found that between January and June 2026, U.S.-based firms bought 78 percent of all AI semiconductors powering data centers and supporting the training of new, more powerful models. EU-based companies purchased just 5 percent of these chips during the same period, paving the way for the trans-Atlantic AI gap to widen further.

Current EU investment metrics make it hard to imagine a more positive outcome for European ambitions in other frontier sectors, such as quantum computing and biotechnology. In the field of quantum technology, data from the Massachusetts Institute of Technology shows that only four EU countries (Finland, France, Germany, and the Netherlands) have funding levels worth noting. Yet together, they accounted for just around 10 percent of global financing in 2025.

Meanwhile, the United States captured nearly two-thirds of worldwide funding for quantum technology, while Britain and Canada—both much smaller economies than the EU—are also notable players. In medical biotech, the pattern is the same: U.S. firms secured 63 percent of global venture capital between January 2015 and June 2025, nine times the 7 percent raised by EU firms, according to the European Commission.

The abysmally low levels of funding for quantum computing and biotech suggest that current European concerns about dependence on the United States for AI are only the start of a long line of similar debates. Time and again, EU firms and consumers end up buying critical technology from elsewhere that they did not finance and develop at home. As the United States’ decision in June to cut off global access to Anthropic’s Fable 5 showed, this gives Washington a simple way to deprive Europe of critical technology.

Trade is the final area in which the EU is reliant on the United States. Consider EU exports. Despite pledges to diversify, European firms have become more dependent on the U.S. market over the past decade. U.S. firms bought 21 percent of the EU’s goods exports in 2025, up from 17 percent in 2015. While a rush to stockpile European goods before the start of U.S. tariffs can partly explain the spike in EU exports to the United States last year, it does not change the long-term trend. Nor is this Europe rerouting exports that China no longer buys; at 8 percent in 2025, China’s share of EU goods exports was unchanged from 2015.

Instead, two factors help explain why European exporters have become increasingly reliant on the U.S. market. The first is pharmaceuticals. Half of the 244 billion euro rise in EU exports to the United States between 2015 and 2025 can be attributed to medicines, such as the Irish-manufactured ingredients of Eli Lilly’s blockbuster obesity drug Mounjaro. In 2025, the United States absorbed 43 percent of EU exports of drugs and medical ingredients, up from 28 percent in 2015. Booming pharmaceutical exports are good news for Europe, but shipping nearly half of them to the United States gives Washington a clear target.

The second factor is that the EU’s pledge to diversify trade ties toward middle powers is materializing only slowly. The share of EU goods exports going to 10 major middle powers, including India, Indonesia, and Turkey, remained unchanged between 2015 and 2025, at only 15 percent.

In terms of imports, the EU’s reliance on the United States is growing, too—particularly in energy and intellectual property (IP). Increased dependence on U.S. energy following Europe’s shift away from Russian supplies is familiar by now: U.S. firms now supply almost two-thirds of EU imports of liquefied natural gas, up from zero in 2015. The lesser-known story has to do with IP. Contrary to Britain, Japan, and the United States, the EU is a net importer of IP, such as software, patents, or trademarks. U.S. firms supply 80 percent of these imports, and the trade is one-sided. Eurostat documents that at 213 billion euros in 2025, the bloc’s imports of U.S. IP have grown sevenfold over the past decade. Last year, the EU bought six times as much IP from the United States as vice versa.

Failed attempts to achieve strategic autonomy, de-risking, and diversification have a precedent. In 2019, Britain, France, and Germany created INSTEX, a barter mechanism intended to maintain European trade with Iran despite U.S. secondary sanctions. INSTEX handled just one transaction before closing in 2023.

Europe’s ambition to achieve economic security stands a better chance of succeeding if Brussels gets serious about creating a euro-denominated safe asset, investing heavily in frontier technology sectors, and doubling down on signing trade deals with middle powers. Delivering on these reforms would be a winning strategy—and not just a succession of buzzwords.

The European Council on Foreign Relations’ report, Interdependence, Revised: A New Dictionary for the World Economy by Agathe Demarais and Chiara Malaponti, was released earlier this month.

Agathe Demarais is a columnist at Foreign Policy, a senior policy fellow on geoeconomics and technology at the European Council on Foreign Relations, a visiting professor at the College of Europe, a former global forecasting director at the Economist Intelligence Unit, and the author of Backfire: How Sanctions Reshape the World Against U.S. Interests. Bluesky: @agathedemarais.com

Amazon workers on food stamps have tripled despite its record revenue—and it’s just the latest evidence of the new economy of shrinking labor shares

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The U.S. workforce has made the type of history it likely wishes it didn’t. Working Americans are taking home the smallest percentage of economic output, 52.8%, since the Bureau of Labor Statistics began tracking the metric in 1947.

But as the share of wealth workers take home through wages—called labor share—is shrinking, corporate profits have exploded, with the S&P 500 index gaining 600% since the beginning of the century, while wages have increased just 12.5% over the same period, adjusted for inflation.

In other words: Corporations are raking in cash, but American workers are reaping less and less of the rewards.

The consequences associated with shrinking labor shares are now becoming tangible. A recent Government Accountability Office (GAO) report found that across the 11 states sampled, Amazon, the country’s largest company by revenue, has 12,346 workers on the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP) and 11,338 relying on Medicaid. That was nearly triple the number of Amazon employees in need of federal assistance compared to 2020, when GAO conducted a similar report. During that same period, Amazon saw an increase in annual profits from $11.6 billion to $77.7 billion. Amazon’s 2025 revenue soared 12% year over year, from $638 billion to a record $717 billion.

Amazon spokesperson Rachael Lighty told Fortune the conclusion drawn from the GAO report is “wrong,” and that it is misleading to look at raw numbers rather than percentages.

“When you look at the actual facts, you see that Amazon pay is among the best in the industry, regular full-time employees have access to health care from their first day—at only $5 per week with $5 copays for employee-only coverage—and 74% of our regular full-time employees are enrolled in an Amazon health insurance plan, well above the 65% private-sector take-up rate for full-time workers,” she said in a statement. “We challenge other large retailers to provide the same Day 1 comprehensive health care benefits that we do.”

Walmart and FedEx saw similar increases in the number of workers taking federal assistance, as did ride-share and delivery companies.

Kathryn Larin, director for education, workforce, and income security issues at GAO, told Fortune the data illustrates that the Americans taking advantage of social safety net programs today are overwhelmingly in the workforce, with most working full-time. The income threshold for SNAP eligibility is about 130% of the poverty line, suggesting that despite many of these workers clocking in at their jobs, they still don’t have enough money to meet their basic needs.

“What this analysis really points to is the large number of people who have very low incomes and continue to have very low income,” Larin said. “I mean, these are families that are really barely able to make ends meet, and yet they are working, and they are working a lot.”

Diane Swonk, chief economist and managing director at KPMG, recently warned of the hidden consequences of a shrinking labor share, particularly that despite economic indicators suggesting the economy is stable, most Americans are facing an ongoing affordability crisis. KPMG found in February that since 1982, corporate profits as a share of U.S. GDP increased from 8% to 15.85%. During that same period, employee compensation as a share of U.S. GDP shrank from 66.6% to 61.9%.

“This chart from my recent Economic Compass still haunts me,” Swonk said in a social media post at the time. “A friend refers to it as the ‘revolution chart,’ which [is] disturbing but telling. Inequality fuels social and economic instability.”

This trend has been about 50 years in the making, according to Anna Stansbury, an assistant professor of work and organization studies at the MIT Sloan School of Management.

Fewer workers in the U.S. are represented by unions—20.1% of U.S. workers in 1983 compared to 10.0% in 2025—giving them fewer opportunities to bargain for salaries and benefits, she noted. But more so, Stansbury blames the fissuring of the workplace, or the breakdown of the employer-employee relationship. In the past, the typical employer-employee relationship would be that of direct employment: A worker for a company does their work at the company they are employed by. For example, a large bank like Bank of America used to employ a janitor to clean its offices.

But “in more and more cases, that’s not actually people’s experience of the workplace, particularly in lower middle-income jobs,” Stansbury told Fortune.

Instead, large workplaces like retailers and banks hire gig workers or subcontractors to complete jobs once done by direct employees: Companies hire a security provider, which employs a security guard to work outside that large company’s office. Delivery drivers are contract workers, not full-time employees.

As a result, companies don’t have to provide those workers equity or benefits. If a subcontractor violates labor law, the company contracting them is not liable. In the meantime, these large companies are not only saving money on benefits, but are also getting to argue that they are increasing efficiency by not spending resources on workers whose roles are not directly driving revenue, Stansbury explained. A bank should employ bankers, not janitors, to get the most bang for its buck, the thinking goes.

Brent Neiman, a professor of economics at the University of Chicago, said he believes AI has been the key driver of diminishing labor shares. In a New York Times op-ed, he argued the technology’s productivity promises pose the greatest threat to wages.

“Anyone who has used ChatGPT can see how much work now done by people could soon be done by technology,” he wrote.

Apollo chief economist Torsten Slok has already observed a correlation between AI adoption and lowered wages. In a white paper published in July, he and economist Sania Edlich found that among 321 occupations between 2015 and 2025, jobs with high exposure to AI saw a 6.7% decline in real wage growth after 2023 (following the wide release of ChatGPT), despite no employment changes, suggesting that while AI is not displacing jobs, it is associated with compressed wages. In other words, companies are seeing increases in productivity, but are using those increases as a cover to raise prices, while keeping wages stagnant.

Stansbury has a slightly different theory about AI. While the technology could begin to have an aggregate impact on the labor market, it’s still too early to say whether today’s shrinking labor share is part of a broader economic cycle, or if it’s a secular event, she said.

Unexpected inflation spikes, like what is currently happening, are usually associated with poorer real wages. If inflation is less volatile in the coming years and wage growth recovers, today’s trend of shrinking labor share could turn out to be cyclical, Stansbury said.

On the other hand, Stansbury noted, a tightening labor market should increase labor share, and the labor market is already relatively tight. If inflation stabilizes, employment remains narrow, and the labor share increases, it will be a sign of an economic cycle completing itself. A bigger problem will be if wage growth stays low even if inflation improves and the labor market stays tight.

“If those two things happen and the labor share continues to fall,” she said, “then it would suggest that there’s actually been a secular shift, a secular acceleration in the downward trend.”

A version of this story was published on Fortune.com on Sept. 13, 2026.

Sasha Rogelberg is a reporter and former editorial fellow on the news desk at Fortune, covering retail and the intersection of business and popular culture.

FIFA confirms cyberattack that accessed personal and ​business information

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

FIFA has experienced a cyberattack, the football association confirmed to Reuters.

On Thursday, the news outlet reported that an external entity had accessed "a limited set of personal and ​business information" within the Fédération Internationale de Football Association. However, FIFA did not detail how many people or files were affected, or when it occurred.

"FIFA can confirm that it has been ​the victim of a criminal cyberattack resulting in ⁠unauthorised access to a limited set of personal and ​business information," a FIFA spokesperson told Reuters in a statement.

A group called the Handsome Boy Hacking Club has claimed responsibility for the cyberattack, according to the BBC, reportedly posting screenshots of FIFA documents online last week (the group has several posts on X declaring themselves the hackers). The news outlet said the group's claimed 12GB of accessed files amounts to less than one percent of FIFA's data. "FIFA condemns in the strongest possible terms the unlawful acquisition and publication of information, particularly personal data," said FIFA in a statement to the BBC. "Such actions constitute a serious and unacceptable violation of privacy and can have significant consequences for the individuals concerned."

A "person with direct knowledge of the matter" added to Reuters that files relating to FIFA's controversial and scrapped Forward Enterprise project (which would have seen embattled FIFA head Gianni Infantino handing over commercial rights for the World Cup) were not accessed.

Marques Brownlee secretly built a new content studio and surprised fans with a first look

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Marques Brownlee has been secretly working on a project, a colossal new studio the content creator unveiled on Wednesday.

A year ago, the Mashable 101 alumnus filmed a walkthrough of his new 30,000-square-foot, two-storey facility under construction, going room-by-room to explain plans for his future video production space.

"This is being recorded in secret, a year ago from when you're watching it," says Brownlee in the video. "The next video you get to watch is the finished studio, which is sick. So this is going to be an empty building tour, and then I'm going to be able to snap my fingers, show you the finished tour, and walk you through it. So you'll see how close I get to my vision in the final thing. I hope I get really close."

Watching creators like Brownlee building their audience and brand to scale up into their own production studios is a delight — and we're more than keen to see the finished space, a video which could drop any day.

Toobit Delivers $474K in Net Slippage Subsidies

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Toobit, the award-winning global cryptocurrency exchange, today reports that its zero-slippage trading service delivered 474,904 USDT in net slippage subsidies between July 1 and October 27, 2026, with 3,766 trading accounts receiving positive subsidies during the period.

Slippage is an often-overlooked trading cost that occurs when an order is filled at a different price than expected. Unlike trading fees, slippage can be difficult to anticipate, particularly during rapid market movements. By reducing unfavorable price differences, traders can better manage their trading costs and keep more of their potential returns.

To help traders address this challenge, Toobit offers its guaranteed price (GTD price) feature for eligible perpetual futures orders. The feature supports take-profit (TP) and stop-loss (SL) settings, with GTD TP targeting fills at the trigger price or better and GTD SL helping protect against unfavorable slippage.

For futures traders, this protection can be particularly valuable during volatile conditions. A conventional stop-loss market order may fill at a price below its trigger when prices fall rapidly, increasing losses beyond the intended level. Guaranteed price helps address this risk by allowing eligible stop-loss orders to close at their preset prices.

Traders can access guaranteed price on supported perpetual futures pairs, subject to eligibility requirements, fees, and execution exceptions. Traders can explore Toobit Futures or learn more through the guaranteed price feature guide.

Toobit is where the future of crypto trading unfolds. The award-winning cryptocurrency derivatives exchange provides zero-fee spot trading, AI trading tools, and high leverage for both crypto and TradFi markets. Built for those who thrive on exploring new frontiers, Toobit maintains a fair, secure, and transparent environment for traders to navigate digital asset markets.

U.S. moves Strategic Bitcoin Reserve bill forward with a 20-year lockup plan

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The U.S. House Financial Services Committee has advanced H.R. 8957, known as the American Reserve Modernization Act of 2026, bringing the proposed legislation one step closer to a full House vote.

Introduced on the 21st of May 2026 by Republican Representative Nick Begich of Alaska, alongside Democratic Representative Jared Golden, the bill seeks to give the U.S. government’s Strategic Bitcoin [BTC] Reserve a formal legal foundation.

It proposes that the U.S. Treasury Department establish two separate structures within 180 days of the legislation becoming law. This includes a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve exclusively for Bitcoin and a Digital Asset Stockpile for other cryptocurrencies held by the federal government.

Under the proposal, Bitcoin owned by the federal government through criminal or civil asset forfeitures and other qualifying sources would be transferred to the Strategic Bitcoin Reserve.

Here, the most significant provision is a 20-year minimum holding requirement, which would prevent the government from selling, swapping, auctioning, or using reserve Bitcoin as collateral during that period. At the same time, the bill also allows the Treasury Department to explore budget-neutral ways of acquiring additional Bitcoin without increasing the national debt.

Separately, the legislation proposes a Digital Asset Stockpile to manage other cryptocurrencies held by the federal government. Lastly, it also introduces requirements for transparency, auditing, and congressional reporting to ensure proper oversight of these assets.

The bill is particularly significant because it seeks to give the U.S. Strategic Bitcoin Reserve a statutory foundation. For its part, President Donald Trump had already established the reserve through an executive order in March 2025.

However, H.R. 8957 aims to formalize its structure and introduce additional safeguards, including the proposed 20-year holding period.

As of now, twenty months have already passed, and the U.S. government reportedly holds around 328,000 BTC, largely from seizures and forfeitures, while governments globally hold about 435,000 BTC.

Henceforth, if H.R. 8957 becomes law, the reserve would receive statutory backing, making it more difficult for a future administration to reverse the policy unilaterally.

The bill received approval from the House Financial Services Committee on 16th September 2026 in a 28-21 vote, with all Republican members voting in favor and all Democratic members opposing it.

Then, on the 24th of November, the committee formally reported the revised legislation to the full House, placing it on the Union Calendar and making it eligible for a floor vote. However, its chances of becoming law remain uncertain.

This is because the 119th Congress ends on the 3rd of January 2027, leaving limited time for the bill to pass the House, clear the Senate, and receive presidential approval.

Adding to this, market expectations also remain pessimistic, with Polymarket traders assigning only a 4% probability to the U.S. establishing a national Bitcoin reserve through legislation before 2027.

All of this coincided with Bitcoin trading at $60,355.92 at press time after a hike of 2.1% in the past 24 hours.

GigaChat 4 Debuts With 1M-Token Context and Two Model Sizes

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The GigaChat Team announced GigaChat 4 on November 26, 2026, releasing 708B- and 37B-parameter mixture-of-experts models with native one-million-token context windows. Both activate only a subset of their parameters per token, support tool calling and multi-step agent tasks, and allow Instant or Reasoning responses to be selected per request within one checkpoint.

The flagship 708B-A41B uses approximately 41B active parameters per token, compared with roughly 3.8B for the compact 37B-A4B. Both were trained on sequences extending to the full context length for work with long documents, collections of sources and large codebases. This arrangement replaces GigaChat 3.5’s separate instant and reasoning checkpoints.

Both models use a 3:1 mix of Gated DeltaNet layers and multi-head latent attention layers enhanced with DeepSeek Sparse Attention (DSA). Gated DeltaNet maintains a fixed-size recurrent state, while DSA selects up to 2,048 earlier positions for each query rather than computing attention over the entire sequence. The companion technical report says indexer and selection overhead can outweigh the attention savings at short contexts or small decode batches.

The flagship has 64 decoder layers and 256 routed experts, selecting eight per token together with one shared expert. The compact model has 32 layers and 192 routed experts, using the same combination of eight routed experts and one shared expert.

For the 708B model, context extension progressed from 32K to 128K tokens before indexer training and joint optimization at the native length. After supervised fine-tuning, specialist models underwent reinforcement learning in mathematics, coding, tool use and multi-turn agent workflows. Multi-teacher on-policy distillation then consolidated their capabilities into one model offering both response modes.

Under the team’s evaluation protocol, the 708B model scored 71.26% on Terminal-Bench 2.0 in Reasoning mode and 60.47% in Instant mode, versus 30.30% and 12.94% respectively for GigaChat 3.5. Its reported Reasoning results also include 80.89% on SWE-bench Verified and 79.55% on BFCL v4, although GLM-5.3 is listed higher at 86.67% on SWE-bench Verified.

The compact model’s reported Reasoning results include 85.00% on LiveCodeBench v6, 70.80% on SWE-bench Verified and 72.00% on BFCL. For the flagship, evaluation combined public runners and scoring code with the team’s own implementations, including seven-hour timeouts for specified agentic benchmarks.

The team’s demonstrations emphasize tasks that chain artifacts and tools: the flagship modified a Mario cartridge’s palette in its binary file and ran the game on a Mac M4, produced an STL vase for 3D printing, and used MCP access to the Cadenza music workstation to compose a track before building an accompanying visualization site.

The flagship model card lists FP8, BF16 and GGUF instruction-tuned variants, along with base and intermediate checkpoints, and releases its instruction-tuned checkpoint under the MIT License. The compact model card lists the same variant categories but uses the Noncommercial License for Individuals 1.0. The detailed technical report currently covers the 708B model; a dedicated report for the 37B model is planned for later.

Both cards document SGLang and vLLM serving, per-request mode selection and OpenAI-compatible tool calling. They recommend Pi for agentic tasks, with Codex CLI and DeepSeek Harness as alternatives for interactive terminal work.

The flagship’s documented FP8 configuration uses two nodes with eight H100 80 GB GPUs each. The compact model’s example uses one H100 80 GB GPU, with approximately 40 GB required for its weights before attention caches, recurrent states and activations.

GigaChat 4 makes deployment terms part of the model choice alongside benchmark scores: the flagship combines MIT licensing with a two-node example, while the compact model offers a single-GPU path under noncommercial terms. For prospective users, the compact checkpoint’s usage restrictions are therefore as consequential as its lower hardware requirement.

nginx security releases of November 2026: am I affected and what to do

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

nginx published security releases on November 18, 2026 (stable 1.30.7, mainline 1.31.9), followed by a smaller one on November 23 (1.30.8 and 1.31.10). If nginx serves your website or sits in front of your application, here is how to check whether you are concerned and what to do.

According to the official nginx security advisories page, the November 18 release fixes several issues, including memory corruption in HTTP/2 flow control (CVE-2026-123007), use-after-free bugs in HTTP/2, HTTP/3, the resolver and the mail proxy module, an infinite loop with TLS 1.3 request bodies, and a buffer overflow with alias and try_files (CVE-2026-125283). The November 23 release fixes excessive CPU usage with ssl_early_data (CVE-2026-132779).

Not every issue applies to every setup. For example, the HTTP/3 issue only matters if you enabled HTTP/3, the mail proxy issue only if you use the mail module, and the ssl_early_data issue only if that directive is on. Your configuration decides what is reachable.

I did not find a primary source confirming attacks in the wild. Check again before assuming either way.

If you are on managed hosting, a CDN or a platform that runs nginx for you, the provider normally patches it. Check their status or security notices.

An outside check cannot reliably read your patch level: many servers hide the nginx version, and that is a good thing. What you can verify for free, without an account, is the surrounding hygiene. The AttackScout public audit reads only public information and covers certificate, email authentication, security headers and more, with an A to F grade. If you want the headers on their own, use the security headers check.

PHP security release of November 19, 2026: am I affected and what to do

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PHP published security releases on November 19, 2026: 8.6.0, 8.5.13, 8.4.28, 8.3.37 and 8.2.36. If your site runs PHP (WordPress, Drupal, Joomla, Laravel and most shared hosting do), here is how to find out whether you are concerned and what to do. No panic needed: this is a routine, but large, patch release.

You are concerned if your server runs PHP below these versions:

Older branches (8.1 and below) no longer receive fixes at all.

The PHP 8.5.13 changelog lists fixes in many components: Core, CGI, Date, DOM, FPM, Phar, Session, SOAP, Standard and URI. Several are memory-safety bugs. One that got attention is CVE-2026-129289, a request smuggling issue in PHP-FPM caused by an unread request body. According to Patchstack, the exposed setup is nginx with fastcgi_keep_conn on in front of PHP-FPM. If you do not use that setting, that specific issue is much less relevant to you, but you should still update.

In the reports I checked, these PHP CVEs were not described as exploited in the wild. That can change, so check again rather than rely on this article.

These are PHP flaws, not WordPress core flaws. Updating WordPress does not fix them; updating PHP does.

An outside check only sees what your server shows publicly. Many servers hide their PHP version, which is good practice, so no external tool can promise your patch level. The reliable answer is php -v on your own server.

What you can check from the outside, for free and without an account, is the rest of the picture: the AttackScout public audit runs up to 112 read-only checks on your domain (email authentication, certificate, security headers, end-of-life software signals) and gives an A to F grade. It is a good way to spot other things that tend to be neglected at the same time as a PHP update.
